```json
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Ichikishimahime adalah dewi air dalam tiga dewi Munakata.",
    "en": "Ichikishimahime is a water goddess among the Munakata trio.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "ichikishimahime-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Ichikishimahime adalah dewi air dalam tiga dewi Munakata. Kronik memakai bentuk penulisan namanya yang berbeda. Ia muncul dalam perlombaan sumpah Amaterasu dan Susanoo.",
      "en": "Ichikishimahime is a water goddess among the Munakata trio. The chronicles use different written forms of her name. She arises in the oath contest between Amaterasu and Susanoo.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "ichikishimahime-c01",
        "ichikishimahime-c02",
        "ichikishimahime-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Pemujaan menghubungkannya dengan kemakmuran, seni, dan perlindungan laut. Kojiki juga menyebutnya Sayoribime. Kojiki menempatkannya di Munakata Nakatsumiya.",
      "en": "Worship connects her with prosperity, arts and maritime protection. The Kojiki also calls her Sayoribime. The Kojiki assigns her to Munakata Nakatsumiya.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "ichikishimahime-c04",
        "ichikishimahime-c05",
        "ichikishimahime-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,

    {
      "id": "ichikishimahime-s1",
      "published": null,
      "accessed": "2026-10-01",
      "url": "https://ja.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E3%82%A4%E3%83%81%E3%82%AD%E3%82%B7%E3%83%9E%E3%83%92%E3%83%A1",
      "title": "イチキシマヒメ",
      "publisher": "Wikipedia",
      "language": "ja",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "author": null
    },
    {
      "id": "ichikishimahime-s2",
      "published": null,
      "accessed": "2026-10-01",
      "url": "https://www.kokugakuin.ac.jp/assets/uploads/2023/03/Kenkyukaihatsusuisinkikou_kiyouNo15.pdf",
      "title": "Kenkyukaihatsusuisinkikou_kiyouNo15.pdf",
      "publisher": "Kokugakuin University",
      "author": null,
      "language": "en",
      "type": "primary-text"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "ichikishimahime-c01",
      "source_id": "ichikishimahime-s1",
      "quote": "イチキシマヒメ（イツキシマヒメとも）は、日本神話に登場する女神で、宗像三女神の一柱。水の神である。『古事記』では市寸島比売命、『日本書紀』では市杵嶋姫命と表記する。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ichikishimahime adalah dewi air dalam tiga dewi Munakata.",
        "en": "Ichikishimahime is a water goddess among the Munakata trio."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ichikishimahime-c02",
      "source_id": "ichikishimahime-s1",
      "quote": "『古事記』では市寸島比売命、『日本書紀』では市杵嶋姫命と表記する。別名、狭依毘売命（さよりひめのみこと）。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kronik memakai bentuk penulisan namanya yang berbeda.",
        "en": "The chronicles use different written forms of her name."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ichikishimahime-c03",
      "source_id": "ichikishimahime-s1",
      "quote": "アマテラスとスサノオが、天眞名井で行った誓約（アマテラスとスサノオの誓約）の際に、アマテラスがスサノオの剣を噛んで吹き出した霧から生まれた三女神の三女。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia muncul dalam perlombaan sumpah Amaterasu dan Susanoo.",
        "en": "She arises in the oath contest between Amaterasu and Susanoo."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ichikishimahime-c04",
      "source_id": "ichikishimahime-s1",
      "quote": "絶世の美女とされ、商売繁盛、芸能、金運、勝負、豊漁、交通安全、五穀豊穣、海の神として信仰されている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pemujaan menghubungkannya dengan kemakmuran, seni, dan perlindungan laut.",
        "en": "Worship connects her with prosperity, arts and maritime protection."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ichikishimahime-c05",
      "source_id": "ichikishimahime-s2",
      "quote": "Next appeared Ichikishimahime no mikoto 市寸嶋比売命 (7). This deity’s other name is Sayoribime no mikoto 狭依毗売命 (8).",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kojiki juga menyebutnya Sayoribime.",
        "en": "The Kojiki also calls her Sayoribime."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ichikishimahime-c06",
      "source_id": "ichikishimahime-s2",
      "quote": "The next, Ichikishimahime no mikoto, is enshrined at the Munakata Nakatsumiya 中津宮 Shrine.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kojiki menempatkannya di Munakata Nakatsumiya.",
        "en": "The Kojiki assigns her to Munakata Nakatsumiya."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
  "gaps": [
    {
      "field": "images",
      "searched": "Belum ada berkas Wikimedia Commons yang diperiksa untuk entri ini."

}
```

```json
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Iki adalah yōkai perasuk dalam tradisi Tiongkok dan Jepang. Kisah Tionghoanya terdapat dalam beberapa kumpulan sastra lama.",
      "en": "Iki is a possessing yōkai in Chinese and Japanese traditions. Its Chinese accounts occur in several old literary collections.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "iki-c01",
        "iki-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Satu kepercayaan mensyaratkan pengganti orang mati sebelum hantu dapat bereinkarnasi. Roh itu konon mencari pengganti yang meninggal dengan cara serupa.",
      "en": "A belief requires a replacement dead person before a ghost can reincarnate. The spirit is said to seek a replacement who dies in the same manner.",

  "cultural_context": null,

  "sources": [
    {
      "id": "iki-s1",
      "published": null,
      "accessed": "2026-10-01",
      "url": "https://ja.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E7%B8%8A%E9%AC%BC",
      "title": "縊鬼",
      "publisher": "Wikipedia",
      "language": "ja",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "author": null
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "iki-c01",
      "source_id": "iki-s1",
      "quote": "縊鬼（いき、いつき、くびれおに）は、中国または日本の妖怪。人に取り憑いて首を括らせるとされる。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Iki adalah yōkai perasuk dalam tradisi Tiongkok dan Jepang.",
        "en": "Iki is a possessing yōkai in Chinese and Japanese traditions."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "iki-c02",
      "source_id": "iki-s1",
      "quote": "中国での縊鬼は「いき」と読み、『小豆棚』『太平御覧』『聊斎志異』などの中国の古書に記述がある。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah Tionghoanya terdapat dalam beberapa kumpulan sastra lama.",
        "en": "Its Chinese accounts occur in several old literary collections."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "iki-c03",
      "source_id": "iki-s1",
      "quote": "中国の伝承においては、冥界には一定の人口が定められており、この人口を常に保つ必要があるため、死者が別の人間として転生して冥界を去ろうにも、自分に代わる後任の死者が冥界に入らなければ、転生の許可が下りない。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Satu kepercayaan mensyaratkan pengganti orang mati sebelum hantu dapat bereinkarnasi.",
        "en": "A belief requires a replacement dead person before a ghost can reincarnate."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "iki-c04",
      "source_id": "iki-s1",
      "quote": "亡者が生まれ変わるには他者に自分と同じ死に方をさせることが条件らしく、縊死した死者が生者に取り憑き、自分と同じように縊死させようとしているものが縊鬼とされる。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Roh itu konon mencari pengganti yang meninggal dengan cara serupa.",
        "en": "The spirit is said to seek a replacement who dies in the same manner."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
```

```json
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Ishinagenjo adalah fenomena lemparan batu misterius dalam cerita rakyat Jepang.",
    "en": "Ishinagenjo is a mysterious stone-throwing phenomenon in Japanese folklore.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "ishinagenjo-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Ishinagenjo adalah fenomena lemparan batu misterius dalam cerita rakyat Jepang. Nelayan mendengar percikan besar yang mengguncang perahu. Tidak ada batu yang terlihat bahkan setelah siang tiba.",
      "en": "Ishinagenjo is a mysterious stone-throwing phenomenon in Japanese folklore. Fishermen hear a huge splash that rocks their boat. No stone is visible even when daylight arrives.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "ishinagenjo-c01",
        "ishinagenjo-c02",
        "ishinagenjo-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Satu tafsir menerjemahkan namanya sebagai perempuan pelempar batu. Kamus justru menafsirkan unsur akhir sebagai kapten atau orang tua. Kamus tersebut menyamakan sosok itu dengan laki-laki tua yang melempar batu ke laut.",
      "en": "One interpretation translates the name as stone-throwing woman. A dictionary instead interprets the ending as captain or old man. That dictionary compares the figure with an old man throwing stones into the sea.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "ishinagenjo-c04",
        "ishinagenjo-c05",
        "ishinagenjo-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,
  "story_mode": null,
  "did_you_know": null,
  "abilities": [],
  "weaknesses": [],
  "variants": [],

      "published": null,

      "title": "Ishinagenjo",
      "publisher": "Wikipedia",
      "language": "en",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "author": null
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "ishinagenjo-c01",
      "source_id": "ishinagenjo-s1",
      "quote": "Ishinagenjo (Japanese: 石投げんじょ, \"stone throwing genjo\") is a folkloric phenomenon",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ishinagenjo adalah fenomena lemparan batu misterius dalam cerita rakyat Jepang.",
        "en": "Ishinagenjo is a mysterious stone-throwing phenomenon in Japanese folklore."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ishinagenjo-c02",
      "source_id": "ishinagenjo-s1",
      "quote": "Suddenly comes the sound of a huge rock crashing into the ocean, a tremendous splash and crack that sends the boat rocking and the sailors panicking.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nelayan mendengar percikan besar yang mengguncang perahu.",
        "en": "Fishermen hear a huge splash that rocks their boat."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ishinagenjo-c03",
      "source_id": "ishinagenjo-s1",
      "quote": "However, there is no rock to be seen, even by the break of day.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tidak ada batu yang terlihat bahkan setelah siang tiba.",
        "en": "No stone is visible even when daylight arrives."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ishinagenjo-c04",
      "source_id": "ishinagenjo-s1",
      "quote": "Written thus, Ishinagenjo (石投女) translates literally to \"stone-throwing woman\".",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Satu tafsir menerjemahkan namanya sebagai perempuan pelempar batu.",
        "en": "One interpretation translates the name as stone-throwing woman."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ishinagenjo-c05",
      "source_id": "ishinagenjo-s1",
      "quote": "The Kojien, on the other hand, renders \"じょ\" as \"尉\", meaning \"captain\" or \"old man\".",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kamus justru menafsirkan unsur akhir sebagai kapten atau orang tua.",
        "en": "A dictionary instead interprets the ending as captain or old man."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ishinagenjo-c06",
      "source_id": "ishinagenjo-s1",
      "quote": "The \"stone-throwing old man\" (石投尉) is likened in the text of the Kojien to an old man idly throwing stones into the sea.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kamus tersebut menyamakan sosok itu dengan laki-laki tua yang melempar batu ke laut.",
        "en": "That dictionary compares the figure with an old man throwing stones into the sea."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [

      "topic": {
        "id": "Tafsir nama",
        "en": "Name interpretation"
      },
```

```json
    },
    {
      "id": "Versi lain menjadikannya anak Iwasaku dan Nesaku. Kojiki mencantumkannya setelah Iwasaku dan Nesaku dalam tiga dewa. Norinaga membedakan Iwasaku dan Nesaku dari Iwatsutsunoo.",
      "en": "Another variant makes him a child of Iwasaku and Nesaku. The Kojiki lists him after Iwasaku and Nesaku among three gods. Norinaga distinguishes Iwasaku and Nesaku from Iwatsutsunoo.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "iwatsutsunoo-c04",
        "iwatsutsunoo-c05",
        "iwatsutsunoo-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],

      "url": "https://kojiki.kokugakuin.ac.jp/wp-content/uploads/2025/06/08-Studies-on-the-Kojiki-Chapter-8-The-Slaying-of-the-Fire-Deity.pdf",
      "title": "08-Studies-on-the-Kojiki-Chapter-8-The-Slaying-of-the-Fire-Deity.pdf",
      "publisher": "Kokugakuin University",
      "author": null,
      "language": "en",
      "type": "primary-text"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "iwatsutsunoo-c01",
      "source_id": "iwatsutsunoo-s1",
      "quote": "イワツツノオ（イハツツノヲ）は、日本神話に登場する神である。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Iwatsutsunoo adalah dewa dalam mitologi Jepang.",
        "en": "Iwatsutsunoo is a deity in Japanese mythology."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "iwatsutsunoo-c02",
      "source_id": "iwatsutsunoo-s1",
      "quote": "『古事記』の神産みの段でイザナギが十拳剣で、妻のイザナミの死因となった火神カグツチの首を斬ったとき、その剣の先についた血が岩について化生した神で、その前に石析神・根析神（磐裂神・根裂神）が化生している。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kojiki menyebutnya muncul ketika darah Kagutsuchi mengenai batu.",
        "en": "The Kojiki has him emerge when Kagutsuchi’s blood strikes rocks."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "iwatsutsunoo-c03",
      "source_id": "iwatsutsunoo-s1",
      "quote": "『日本書紀』同段の第六の一書も同様で、ここでは磐筒男神は経津主神の祖であると記されている。『日本書紀』同段の第七の一書では、磐裂神・根裂神の子として磐筒男神・磐筒女神が生まれたとし、この両神の子が経津主神であるとしている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Versi Nihon Shoki menghubungkannya dengan leluhur Futsunushi.",
        "en": "Nihon Shoki variants associate him with Futsunushi’s ancestry."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "iwatsutsunoo-c04",
      "source_id": "iwatsutsunoo-s1",
      "quote": "『日本書紀』同段の第七の一書では、磐裂神・根裂神の子として磐筒男神・磐筒女神が生まれたとし、この両神の子が経津主神であるとしている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Versi lain menjadikannya anak Iwasaku dan Nesaku.",
        "en": "Another variant makes him a child of Iwasaku and Nesaku."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "iwatsutsunoo-c05",
      "source_id": "iwatsutsunoo-s2",
      "quote": "The deity that thereupon came into existence was named Iwasaku no kami 石析神. Next [appeared] Nesaku no kami 根析神. Next Iwatsutsunoo no kami 石箇之男神 (6). [Gloss:] Three deities.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kojiki mencantumkannya setelah Iwasaku dan Nesaku dalam tiga dewa.",
        "en": "The Kojiki lists him after Iwasaku and Nesaku among three gods."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "iwatsutsunoo-c06",
      "source_id": "iwatsutsunoo-s2",
      "quote": "Norinaga treats Iwasaku no kami and Nesaku no kami separately from Iwatsutsunoo no kami.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Norinaga membedakan Iwasaku dan Nesaku dari Iwatsutsunoo.",
        "en": "Norinaga distinguishes Iwasaku and Nesaku from Iwatsutsunoo."
      }
    }
  ],
```

```json
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Jakotsu-baba adalah yōkai dalam kumpulan ilustrasi Sekien.",
    "en": "Jakotsu-baba is a yōkai in Sekien’s illustrated collection.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "jakotsu-baba-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Jakotsu-baba adalah yōkai dalam kumpulan ilustrasi Sekien. Gambar memperlihatkan perempuan tua yang dililit ular besar. Sekien menduga adanya hubungan dengan negeri dalam mitologi Tiongkok.",
      "en": "Jakotsu-baba is a yōkai in Sekien’s illustrated collection. The image shows an old woman entwined with large snakes. Sekien speculates about a connection with a country in Chinese mythology.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "jakotsu-baba-c01",
        "jakotsu-baba-c02",
        "jakotsu-baba-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Teks menyebutnya istri Jagoemon tanpa menentukan sumbernya. Kisah belakangan memberinya ular biru di tangan kanan dan ular merah di tangan kiri. Uraian belakangan mengakui bahwa pencerita berspekulasi tentang perilakunya.",
      "en": "The text calls her Jagoemon’s wife without specifying the source. A later account gives her a blue snake in her right hand and a red one in her left. The later account acknowledges that storytellers speculate about her behavior.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "jakotsu-baba-c04",
        "jakotsu-baba-c05",
        "jakotsu-baba-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,
  "story_mode": null,

    {

  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "jakotsu-baba-c01",
      "source_id": "jakotsu-baba-s1",
      "quote": "蛇骨婆（じゃこつばばあ）または蛇五婆（じゃごばあ）は鳥山石燕の『今昔百鬼拾遺』にある妖怪。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Jakotsu-baba adalah yōkai dalam kumpulan ilustrasi Sekien.",
        "en": "Jakotsu-baba is a yōkai in Sekien’s illustrated collection."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "jakotsu-baba-c02",
      "source_id": "jakotsu-baba-s1",
      "quote": "絵では体に大蛇を巻きつけた老婆の姿で描かれており、鳥山石燕の『今昔百鬼拾遺』（1781年刊、右図参照）の解説文には以下のようにある。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Gambar memperlihatkan perempuan tua yang dililit ular besar.",
        "en": "The image shows an old woman entwined with large snakes."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "jakotsu-baba-c03",
      "source_id": "jakotsu-baba-s1",
      "quote": "「もろこし」（唐土）とは中国大陸のこと、「巫咸国」とは中国の地理書『山海経』海外西経にある地で、右手に青蛇、左手に赤蛇を持っている人がいるとされている、石燕は「未詳」としつつ、「蛇を持っている」ところから蛇骨婆はこの巫咸国の住人なのではないかと解説している。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sekien menduga adanya hubungan dengan negeri dalam mitologi Tiongkok.",
        "en": "Sekien speculates about a connection with a country in Chinese mythology."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "jakotsu-baba-c04",
      "source_id": "jakotsu-baba-s1",
      "quote": "また、蛇骨婆は「蛇五右衛門」の妻だと述べているが、その根拠を「或説に云」としか述べておらず、どのような文献によるものかは判明していない。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Teks menyebutnya istri Jagoemon tanpa menentukan sumbernya.",
        "en": "The text calls her Jagoemon’s wife without specifying the source."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "jakotsu-baba-c05",
      "source_id": "jakotsu-baba-s2",
      "quote": "She is described as carrying a blue snake in her right hand and a red snake in her left hand.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah belakangan memberinya ular biru di tangan kanan dan ular merah di tangan kiri.",
        "en": "A later account gives her a blue snake in her right hand and a red one in her left."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "jakotsu-baba-c06",
      "source_id": "jakotsu-baba-s2",
      "quote": "her behavior is the subject of speculation by storytellers.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Uraian belakangan mengakui bahwa pencerita berspekulasi tentang perilakunya.",
        "en": "The later account acknowledges that storytellers speculate about her behavior."
      }
```

```json
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Janjanbi adalah cahaya gaib dalam legenda Nara. Ia dianggap sebagai jenis onibi. Namanya meniru bunyi yang dihasilkannya.",
      "en": "Janjanbi is a ghost light in Nara legends. It is regarded as a type of onibi. Its name imitates the sound it makes.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "janjanbi-c01",
        "janjanbi-c02",
        "janjanbi-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Tradisi dan namanya beragam menurut tempat. Versi Nara terdiri dari dua bola api di pemakaman. Mereka bertemu di sungai lalu kembali ke makamnya.",
      "en": "Its traditions and names vary by locality. A Nara version consists of two fireballs at graveyards. They meet by a river and return to their graves.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "janjanbi-c04",
        "janjanbi-c05",
        "janjanbi-c06"
      ]

  "claims": [

      "id": "janjanbi-c01",
      "source_id": "janjanbi-s1",
      "quote": "Janjanbi (じゃんじゃん火 or ジャンジャン火) is an atmospheric ghost light told about the legends of various places in the Nara Prefecture.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Janjanbi adalah cahaya gaib dalam legenda Nara.",
        "en": "Janjanbi is a ghost light in Nara legends."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "janjanbi-c02",
      "source_id": "janjanbi-s1",
      "quote": "They are considered a type of onibi. In the Miyazaki Prefecture they are also called musabi",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia dianggap sebagai jenis onibi.",
        "en": "It is regarded as a type of onibi."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "janjanbi-c03",
      "source_id": "janjanbi-s1",
      "quote": "Their name comes from the \"janjan\" sound that they make.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Namanya meniru bunyi yang dihasilkannya.",
        "en": "Its name imitates the sound it makes."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "janjanbi-c04",
      "source_id": "janjanbi-s1",
      "quote": "Also in Nara, there are different legends depending on the area, and different alternate names depending on the area.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tradisi dan namanya beragam menurut tempat.",
        "en": "Its traditions and names vary by locality."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "janjanbi-c05",
      "source_id": "janjanbi-s1",
      "quote": "Byakugō-ji town, Nara city It indicates two balls of fire that appear in the graveyard of Byakugō-ji and Daian-ji.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Versi Nara terdiri dari dua bola api di pemakaman.",
        "en": "A Nara version consists of two fireballs at graveyards."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "janjanbi-c06",
      "source_id": "janjanbi-s1",
      "quote": "In the Fūfu River, the two balls of fire would meet together, intertwine themselves, and then finally return to their graves.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mereka bertemu di sungai lalu kembali ke makamnya.",
        "en": "They meet by a river and return to their graves."
      }
    }
  ],
```

```json
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Kaichigo adalah yōkai dalam buku benda hidup karya Sekien. Gambarnya menyerupai anak yang merangkak keluar dari wadah kerang. Tafsir peneliti menganggapnya roh kerang ciptaan Sekien.",
      "en": "Kaichigo is a yōkai in Sekien’s book of animated objects. Its childlike image crawls from a shell container. A scholarly interpretation treats it as Sekien’s invented shell spirit.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "kaichigo-c01",
        "kaichigo-c02",
        "kaichigo-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Tafsir lain menghubungkannya dengan kotak kerang perlengkapan pengantin yang diwariskan. Kisah belakangan menggambarkan roh kotak kerang sebagai anak kecil berkimono. Kisah itu menyebutnya bermain dengan kerang ketika tidak ada orang.",

      "published": null,

      "url": "https://ja.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E8%B2%9D%E5%85%90",
      "title": "貝児",
      "publisher": "Wikipedia",
      "language": "ja",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "author": null
    },
    {
      "id": "kaichigo-s2",
      "published": null,
      "accessed": "2026-10-01",
      "url": "https://yokai.com/kaichigo/",
      "title": "Kaichigo",
      "publisher": "Yokai.com",
      "language": "en",
      "type": "encyclopedia",
      "author": "Matthew Meyer"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "kaichigo-c01",
      "source_id": "kaichigo-s1",
      "quote": "貝児（かいちご）は、鳥山石燕の妖怪画集『百器徒然袋』にある妖怪。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kaichigo adalah yōkai dalam buku benda hidup karya Sekien.",
        "en": "Kaichigo is a yōkai in Sekien’s book of animated objects."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kaichigo-c02",
      "source_id": "kaichigo-s1",
      "quote": "画図では貝桶から子供のような姿の者が這い出る様子が描かれている。貝桶とは、日本の中世から江戸時代にかけて伝わる遊戯の貝合わせや貝覆いに用いる貝殻の入れ物であり、解説文には「この貝児は這子の兄弟にやと、おぼつかなく夢心に思ひぬ」とあるが、這子とは幼児のお守りに使われた四つんばい姿の幼児の人形のことで、石燕は貝児をその這子の兄弟かとしている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Gambarnya menyerupai anak yang merangkak keluar dari wadah kerang.",
        "en": "Its childlike image crawls from a shell container."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kaichigo-c03",
      "source_id": "kaichigo-s1",
      "quote": "この貝児が出現したという明確な伝承は残されていないため、貝合わせの貝が妖怪化したものとして石燕が創作したと考えられている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tafsir peneliti menganggapnya roh kerang ciptaan Sekien.",
        "en": "A scholarly interpretation treats it as Sekien’s invented shell spirit."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kaichigo-c04",
      "source_id": "kaichigo-s1",
      "quote": "また、かつて貝桶は母親から娘へ贈られる嫁入り道具の一つとして珍重されており、古来の嫁入り道具は親から子へと受け継がれ、数百年も伝わっているものも珍しくなかったことから、歳月を経た嫁入り道具の貝桶から生まれたものという解釈もある。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tafsir lain menghubungkannya dengan kotak kerang perlengkapan pengantin yang diwariskan.",
        "en": "Another interpretation connects it with inherited bridal shell boxes."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kaichigo-c05",
      "source_id": "kaichigo-s2",
      "quote": "Kaichigo is the spirit of a shell box come to life. It takes the form of a small, doll-like boy in a kimono.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah belakangan menggambarkan roh kotak kerang sebagai anak kecil berkimono.",
        "en": "A later account depicts the shell-box spirit as a small boy in a kimono."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kaichigo-c06",
      "source_id": "kaichigo-s2",
      "quote": "They come out when nobody is around and play with the shells, flipping them over and moving them around into different positions.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah itu menyebutnya bermain dengan kerang ketika tidak ada orang.",
        "en": "That account says it plays with the shells when nobody is present."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
  "gaps": [
    {
      "field": "images",
      "searched": "Belum ada berkas Wikimedia Commons yang diperiksa untuk entri ini."
    }
  ]
}
```

```json
      "id": "Kamo Wakeikazuchi adalah dewa Shinto yang dipuja di Kuil Kamigamo. Ia tidak muncul dalam Kojiki dan Nihon Shoki, tetapi teks abad pertengahan menyamakannya dengan Ajisukitakahikone.",
      "en": "Kamo Wakeikazuchi is a Shinto deity enshrined at Kamigamo Shrine. He is absent from the Kojiki and Nihon Shoki but a medieval text equates him with Ajisukitakahikone.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "kamo-wakeikazuchi-c01",
        "kamo-wakeikazuchi-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Legenda kelahiran menyebut Tamayorihime mengandung setelah membawa pulang panah merah. Saat jamuan ia naik menembus atap sehingga ayah ilahinya diketahui.",
      "en": "The birth legend has Tamayorihime conceive after bringing home a red arrow. At a feast he ascends through the roof, revealing his divine father.",

      ]

  ],
  "cultural_context": null,

      "title": "賀茂別雷命",
      "publisher": "Wikipedia",
      "language": "ja",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "author": null
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "kamo-wakeikazuchi-c01",
      "source_id": "kamo-wakeikazuchi-s1",
      "quote": "賀茂別雷命（かもわけいかづちのみこと）は、神道の神である。 == 概要 == 賀茂別雷神社（上賀茂神社）の祭神であり、各地の加茂神社（賀茂神社・鴨神社）で祀られる。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kamo Wakeikazuchi adalah dewa Shinto yang dipuja di Kuil Kamigamo.",
        "en": "Kamo Wakeikazuchi is a Shinto deity enshrined at Kamigamo Shrine."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kamo-wakeikazuchi-c02",
      "source_id": "kamo-wakeikazuchi-s1",
      "quote": "古事記と日本書紀には登場しない。室町時代の『賀茂之本地』では、記紀に出てくる阿遅鉏高日子根神と同一視されている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia tidak muncul dalam Kojiki dan Nihon Shoki, tetapi teks abad pertengahan menyamakannya dengan Ajisukitakahikone.",
        "en": "He is absent from the Kojiki and Nihon Shoki but a medieval text equates him with Ajisukitakahikone."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kamo-wakeikazuchi-c03",
      "source_id": "kamo-wakeikazuchi-s1",
      "quote": "それを持ち帰って寝床の近くに置いたところ玉依姫は懐妊し、男の子が生まれた。これが賀茂別雷命である。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Legenda kelahiran menyebut Tamayorihime mengandung setelah membawa pulang panah merah.",
        "en": "The birth legend has Tamayorihime conceive after bringing home a red arrow."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kamo-wakeikazuchi-c04",
      "source_id": "kamo-wakeikazuchi-s1",
      "quote": "賀茂別雷命が成人し、その祝宴の席で祖父の賀茂建角身命が「汝の父と思はむ人に此の酒を飮ましめよ（お前のお父さんにもこの酒をあげなさい）」と言ったところ、賀茂別雷命は屋根を突き抜け天に昇っていったので、この子の父が神であることがわかったという。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Saat jamuan ia naik menembus atap sehingga ayah ilahinya diketahui.",
        "en": "At a feast he ascends through the roof, revealing his divine father."
      }
    }
  ],

      }
```

```json
    {

      "locator": "Article text",
```

```json
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Kanedama adalah roh uang Jepang yang konon memakmurkan rumah. Sekien menggambarkan koin memenuhi gudang rumah yang berbuat baik.",
      "en": "Kanedama is a Japanese money spirit said to enrich a household. Sekien depicts coins filling the storehouse of a virtuous household.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "kanedama-c01",
        "kanedama-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Satu tafsir menganggap gambar itu lambang ganjaran bagi kebajikan. Versi Kyōden mengunjungi orang jujur dan meninggalkan mereka yang dikuasai keserakahan.",
      "en": "One interpretation treats the image as a symbol of rewards for virtue. Kyōden’s version visits honest people and leaves those overcome by greed.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "kanedama-c03",
        "kanedama-c04"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,
  "story_mode": null,
  "did_you_know": null,
  "abilities": [],
  "weaknesses": [],
  "variants": [],
  "stories": [],
  "places": [],
  "timeline": [],
  "relations": [],
  "modern_depictions": [],
  "tradition_vs_modern": null,
  "learning_questions": [],
  "images": [],
  "sources": [
    {
      "id": "kanedama-s1",
      "published": null,
      "accessed": "2026-10-01",
      "url": "https://ja.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E9%87%91%E9%9C%8A",
      "title": "金霊",
      "publisher": "Wikipedia",
      "language": "ja",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "author": null
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "kanedama-c01",
      "source_id": "kanedama-s1",
      "quote": "金霊（金玉、かねだま、かなだま）は、日本に伝わる金の精霊、または金の気。 厳密には金霊と金玉は似て非なるものだが、訪れた家を栄えさせるという共通点があり、金玉が金霊の名で伝承されていることもある。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kanedama adalah roh uang Jepang yang konon memakmurkan rumah.",
        "en": "Kanedama is a Japanese money spirit said to enrich a household."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kanedama-c02",
      "source_id": "kanedama-s1",
      "quote": "鳥山石燕による江戸時代の妖怪画集『今昔画図続百鬼』によれば、善行に努める家に金霊が現れ、土蔵が大判小判であふれる様子が描かれている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sekien menggambarkan koin memenuhi gudang rumah yang berbuat baik.",
        "en": "Sekien depicts coins filling the storehouse of a virtuous household."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kanedama-c03",
      "source_id": "kanedama-s1",
      "quote": "これらのことから石燕の金霊の絵は、実際に金霊というものが家に現れるのではなく、無欲善行の者に福が訪れることを象徴したものとされている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Satu tafsir menganggap gambar itu lambang ganjaran bagi kebajikan.",
        "en": "One interpretation treats the image as a symbol of rewards for virtue."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kanedama-c04",
      "source_id": "kanedama-s1",
      "quote": "1803年（享和3年）の山東京伝による草双紙『怪談摸摸夢字彙（かいだんももんじい）』では「金玉（かねだま）」の名で記載されており、正直者のもとに飛び込み、欲に溺れると去るものとされている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Versi Kyōden mengunjungi orang jujur dan meninggalkan mereka yang dikuasai keserakahan.",
        "en": "Kyōden’s version visits honest people and leaves those overcome by greed."
      }
    }
  ],
```

```json
    "claim_ids": [
```
```json
      "kejoro-c01"

  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Kejōrō adalah yōkai dalam buku ilustrasi Sekien dan fiksi zaman Edo. Ia menyerupai pelacur dengan rambut yang menutupi wajahnya. Adam Kabat justru menafsirkannya tidak memiliki wajah sama sekali.",
      "en": "Kejōrō is a yōkai in Sekien’s illustrated books and Edo fiction. She resembles a courtesan with hair obscuring her face. Adam Kabat instead interprets her as having no face at all.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "kejoro-c01",
        "kejoro-c02",
        "kejoro-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Katsumi Tada menafsirkan sosok itu sebagai sindiran terhadap Yoshiwara. Kisah belakangan menyebutnya tampak seperti perempuan biasa dari belakang. Uraian belakangan mengakui ketidakpastian mengenai wajahnya yang tertutup.",
      "en": "Katsumi Tada interprets the figure as satire of Yoshiwara. The later account says she resembles an ordinary woman from behind. The later account acknowledges uncertainty over her concealed face.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "kejoro-c04",
        "kejoro-c05",
        "kejoro-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "kejoro-c01",
      "source_id": "kejoro-s1",
      "quote": "毛倡妓、毛女郎（けじょうろう）は、鳥山石燕の妖怪画集『今昔画図続百鬼』や江戸時代の黄表紙にある日本の妖怪。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kejōrō adalah yōkai dalam buku ilustrasi Sekien dan fiksi zaman Edo.",
        "en": "Kejōrō is a yōkai in Sekien’s illustrated books and Edo fiction."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kejoro-c02",
      "source_id": "kejoro-s1",
      "quote": "その名の通り、長い髪がぼうぼうの倡妓（遊女）の姿をしており、遊廓に現れるとされる。 == 概要 == 『今昔画図続百鬼』では、顔も見えないほど毛むくじゃらの倡妓の姿で描かれている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia menyerupai pelacur dengan rambut yang menutupi wajahnya.",
        "en": "She resembles a courtesan with hair obscuring her face."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kejoro-c03",
      "source_id": "kejoro-s1",
      "quote": "日本文学研究家・アダム・カバットはこの妖怪を、長い髪に隠れて顔が見えないのではなく、最初から顔のない、のっぺらぼうのような妖怪としている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Adam Kabat justru menafsirkannya tidak memiliki wajah sama sekali.",
        "en": "Adam Kabat instead interprets her as having no face at all."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kejoro-c04",
      "source_id": "kejoro-s1",
      "quote": "妖怪研究家・多田克己はこれを、江戸時代の吉原遊廓を風刺した創作と指摘している。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Katsumi Tada menafsirkan sosok itu sebagai sindiran terhadap Yoshiwara.",
        "en": "Katsumi Tada interprets the figure as satire of Yoshiwara."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kejoro-c05",
      "source_id": "kejoro-s2",
      "quote": "From behind they look just like normal women with their hair hanging down loosely.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah belakangan menyebutnya tampak seperti perempuan biasa dari belakang.",
        "en": "The later account says she resembles an ordinary woman from behind."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kejoro-c06",
      "source_id": "kejoro-s2",
      "quote": "His original description is unclear as to whether the kejōrō has normal facial features under the matte of hair which completely covers her face, or whether she is a faceless monster",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
```

```json
  "countries": null,

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Kenas-unarpe adalah kamuy jahat dalam tradisi Ainu.",
    "en": "Kenas-unarpe is an evil kamuy in Ainu tradition.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "kenas-unarpe-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Kenas-unarpe adalah kamuy jahat dalam tradisi Ainu. Ia muncul sebagai nenek gunung berambut kusut atau burung mengerikan. Asal-usul yang berbeda menghubungkannya dengan alat api yang gagal atau kapak yang dibuang.",
      "en": "Kenas-unarpe is an evil kamuy in Ainu tradition. She appears as a mountain hag with tangled hair or a monstrous bird. Different origins associate her with a failed fire tool or discarded axes.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "kenas-unarpe-c01",
        "kenas-unarpe-c02",
        "kenas-unarpe-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia memakai beruang sebagai pengikut dan menipu pemburu. Namanya ditafsirkan sebagai bibi dataran berhutan atau semak berawa. Nitat-unarpe adalah nama lain yang berkaitan dengan rawa.",
      "en": "She employs bears as minions and deceives hunters. Her name is glossed as aunt of the woodland plain or swampy bush. Nitat-unarpe is another name associated with swamps.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "kenas-unarpe-c04",
        "kenas-unarpe-c05",
        "kenas-unarpe-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,
  "story_mode": null,
  "did_you_know": null,
  "abilities": [],
  "weaknesses": [],
  "variants": [],
  "stories": [],
  "places": [],
  "timeline": [],
  "relations": [],
  "modern_depictions": [],
  "tradition_vs_modern": null,
  "learning_questions": [],
  "images": [],
  "sources": [
    {
      "id": "kenas-unarpe-s1",
      "published": null,
      "accessed": "2026-10-01",
      "url": "https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kenas-unarpe",
      "title": "Kenas-unarpe",
      "publisher": "Wikipedia",
      "language": "en",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "author": null
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "kenas-unarpe-c01",
      "source_id": "kenas-unarpe-s1",
      "quote": "Kenas-unarpe (ケナㇱウナㇻペ) is an Ainu wen kamuy (evil god).",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kenas-unarpe adalah kamuy jahat dalam tradisi Ainu.",
        "en": "Kenas-unarpe is an evil kamuy in Ainu tradition."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kenas-unarpe-c02",
      "source_id": "kenas-unarpe-s1",
      "quote": "She is described as akin to a mountain hag (yama-uba) with matted hair, or a monstrous bird.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia muncul sebagai nenek gunung berambut kusut atau burung mengerikan.",
        "en": "She appears as a mountain hag with tangled hair or a monstrous bird."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kenas-unarpe-c03",
      "source_id": "kenas-unarpe-s1",
      "quote": "An origin myth says that she was born from the creator god's failed attempt at crafting the first fire making tool, another version says she spawned from the god's used and discarded axes that rotted into mire.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Asal-usul yang berbeda menghubungkannya dengan alat api yang gagal atau kapak yang dibuang.",
        "en": "Different origins associate her with a failed fire tool or discarded axes."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kenas-unarpe-c04",
      "source_id": "kenas-unarpe-s1",
      "quote": "She is also described as using bears (or other animals turned bear) as minions for her misdeeds, including the killing of livestock or humans, and she is a trickster especially to the hunter.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia memakai beruang sebagai pengikut dan menipu pemburu.",
        "en": "She employs bears as minions and deceives hunters."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kenas-unarpe-c05",
      "source_id": "kenas-unarpe-s1",
      "quote": "She is called Kenas-unarpe (\"aunt of the woodland plain\" or \"aunt of the swampy bush\") and called Kenas-kor-unarpe in the Saru District or Horobetsu region in former Iburi Province (present-day Noboribetsu).",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Namanya ditafsirkan sebagai bibi dataran berhutan atau semak berawa.",
        "en": "Her name is glossed as aunt of the woodland plain or swampy bush."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kenas-unarpe-c06",
      "source_id": "kenas-unarpe-s1",
      "quote": "She is otherwise known as Nitat-unarpe (\"aunt of the swamps\" or \"marshes\").",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nitat-unarpe adalah nama lain yang berkaitan dengan rawa.",
        "en": "Nitat-unarpe is another name associated with swamps."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
  "gaps": [
    {
      "field": "images",
```

```json
  "countries": null,

  "traits": [],
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Kosenjōbi adalah cahaya gaib Jepang yang dicatat dalam buku zaman Edo.",
    "en": "Kosenjōbi is a Japanese ghost light recorded in Edo books.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "kosenjobi-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Kosenjōbi adalah cahaya gaib Jepang yang dicatat dalam buku zaman Edo. Cahaya itu dikaitkan dengan roh tentara dan hewan yang tewas dalam pertempuran. Sekien menyebut cahaya itu timbul dari darah yang tertumpah di tanah.",
      "en": "Kosenjōbi is a Japanese ghost light recorded in Edo books. The lights are attributed to spirits of soldiers and animals killed in battle. Sekien says the lights arise from blood spilled on the ground.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "kosenjobi-c01",
        "kosenjobi-c02",
        "kosenjobi-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Mereka konon terbang berkeliling tanpa langsung menyakiti orang. Uraian belakangan menggambarkan tak terhitung nyala api yang melayang tanpa arah. Uraian itu juga memberi mereka bentuk tentara dan hewan yang terluka.",
      "en": "They reportedly fly about without directly harming people. A later description presents countless aimlessly floating flames. That description also gives them forms of wounded warriors and animals.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "kosenjobi-c04",
        "kosenjobi-c05",
        "kosenjobi-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,
  "story_mode": null,
  "did_you_know": null,
  "abilities": [],
  "weaknesses": [],
  "variants": [],
  "stories": [],
  "places": [],
  "timeline": [],
  "relations": [],
  "modern_depictions": [],
  "tradition_vs_modern": null,
  "learning_questions": [],
  "images": [],
  "sources": [
    {
      "id": "kosenjobi-s1",
      "published": null,
      "accessed": "2026-10-01",
      "url": "https://ja.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E5%8F%A4%E6%88%A6%E5%A0%B4%E7%81%AB",
      "title": "古戦場火",
      "publisher": "Wikipedia",
      "language": "ja",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "author": null
    },
    {
      "id": "kosenjobi-s2",
      "published": null,
      "accessed": "2026-10-01",
      "url": "https://yokai.com/kosenjoubi/",
      "title": "Kosenjōbi",
      "publisher": "Yokai.com",
      "language": "en",
      "type": "encyclopedia",
      "author": "Matthew Meyer"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "kosenjobi-c01",
      "source_id": "kosenjobi-s1",
      "quote": "古戦場火（こせんじょうび）または古戦場の火（こせんじょうのひ）は、日本の伝承にある鬼火の一種。鳥山石燕の妖怪画集『今昔画図続百鬼』や怪談集『宿直草』などの江戸時代の古書に記述がある。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kosenjōbi adalah cahaya gaib Jepang yang dicatat dalam buku zaman Edo.",
        "en": "Kosenjōbi is a Japanese ghost light recorded in Edo books."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kosenjobi-c02",
      "source_id": "kosenjobi-s1",
      "quote": "多くの人間が死んだ戦場に、数え切れないほどの鬼火の集団となって現れ、ふわふわと宙をさまよう。戦場で命を落とした兵士や動物の怨霊とされている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Cahaya itu dikaitkan dengan roh tentara dan hewan yang tewas dalam pertempuran.",
        "en": "The lights are attributed to spirits of soldiers and animals killed in battle."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kosenjobi-c03",
      "source_id": "kosenjobi-s1",
      "quote": "『今昔画図続百鬼』では、死者の血が地面に滴り、そこから発生するとされている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sekien menyebut cahaya itu timbul dari darah yang tertumpah di tanah.",
        "en": "Sekien says the lights arise from blood spilled on the ground."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kosenjobi-c04",
      "source_id": "kosenjobi-s1",
      "quote": "成仏できない怨霊が生者に害を成す話は多いものの、古戦場火は人に害を成すことなく、ただ宙を飛び回るだけと言われているが、これに遭遇した人は念仏を唱えながら帰ったという。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mereka konon terbang berkeliling tanpa langsung menyakiti orang.",
        "en": "They reportedly fly about without directly harming people."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kosenjobi-c05",
      "source_id": "kosenjobi-s2",
      "quote": "Kosenjōbi appear as countless orbs of flame which float about aimlessly through the air.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Uraian belakangan menggambarkan tak terhitung nyala api yang melayang tanpa arah.",
        "en": "A later description presents countless aimlessly floating flames."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kosenjobi-c06",
      "source_id": "kosenjobi-s2",
      "quote": "Kosenjōbi occasionally take on the form of wounded warriors and animals.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Uraian itu juga memberi mereka bentuk tentara dan hewan yang terluka.",
        "en": "That description also gives them forms of wounded warriors and animals."
      }
    }
  ],
```

```json
  "countries": null,

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Kuzuryū adalah dewa naga berkepala sembilan dari Jepang.",
    "en": "Kuzuryū is a Japanese nine-headed dragon deity.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "kuzuryu-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Kuzuryū adalah dewa naga berkepala sembilan dari Jepang. Naga itu biasanya dikaitkan dengan air. Legenda Hakone menempatkan naga penuntut kurban di Danau Ashi. Pendeta Mankan konon menundukkan dan merantai naga itu.",
      "en": "Kuzuryū is a Japanese nine-headed dragon deity. The dragon is commonly associated with water. The Hakone legend places a dragon demanding sacrifices in Lake Ashi. The priest Mankan is said to subdue and chain the dragon.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "kuzuryu-c01",
        "kuzuryu-c02",
        "kuzuryu-c03",
        "kuzuryu-c04"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Legenda itu menghasilkan pemujaan sebagai Kuzuryū Daimyōjin. Persembahan dalam legenda tersebut berubah menjadi nasi dengan kacang merah. Kisah belakangan memberi para naga kuasa atas hujan dan air. Uraian belakangan menekankan perbedaan antartadisi setempat.",
      "en": "The legend leads to worship as Kuzuryū Daimyōjin. Offerings in that legend change to rice cooked with red beans. A later account assigns the dragons power over rain and water. The later account stresses differences among local traditions.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "kuzuryu-c05",
        "kuzuryu-c06",
        "kuzuryu-c07",
        "kuzuryu-c08"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,
  "story_mode": null,
  "did_you_know": null,
  "abilities": [],

    {
      "id": "kuzuryu-s1",
      "published": null,

    },
    {
      "id": "kuzuryu-s2",
      "published": null,

      "publisher": "Yokai.com",
      "language": "en",
      "type": "encyclopedia",
      "author": "Matthew Meyer"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "kuzuryu-c01",
      "source_id": "kuzuryu-s1",
      "quote": "Kuzuryū (九頭龍, nine-headed dragon), or Kuzuryūshin (九頭龍神, nine-headed dragon god) is a deity that appears in folklore and legends of various parts of Japan, including, for example, Hakone (Kanagawa), Nagano Prefecture and Fukui Prefecture.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kuzuryū adalah dewa naga berkepala sembilan dari Jepang.",
        "en": "Kuzuryū is a Japanese nine-headed dragon deity."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kuzuryu-c02",
      "source_id": "kuzuryu-s1",
      "quote": "In many cases, the Kuzuryū is associated with water.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Naga itu biasanya dikaitkan dengan air.",
        "en": "The dragon is commonly associated with water."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kuzuryu-c03",
      "source_id": "kuzuryu-s1",
      "quote": "During the Nara period, a legend arose that the Nine-headed Dragon had settled in Lake Ashi in Hakone, and that it demanded a sacrifice.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Legenda Hakone menempatkan naga penuntut kurban di Danau Ashi.",
        "en": "The Hakone legend places a dragon demanding sacrifices in Lake Ashi."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kuzuryu-c04",
      "source_id": "kuzuryu-s1",
      "quote": "A priest named Mankan (Japanese: 万巻上人) cursed the dragon, and is said to have chained it to the Upside-down Cedar (an underwater rock formation).",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pendeta Mankan konon menundukkan dan merantai naga itu.",
        "en": "The priest Mankan is said to subdue and chain the dragon."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kuzuryu-c05",
      "source_id": "kuzuryu-s1",
      "quote": "As a result of this legend, the dragon came to be worshipped as Kuzuryū Daimyōjin (九頭竜大明神, \"Great God Nine-Headed Dragon).",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Legenda itu menghasilkan pemujaan sebagai Kuzuryū Daimyōjin.",
        "en": "The legend leads to worship as Kuzuryū Daimyōjin."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kuzuryu-c06",
      "source_id": "kuzuryu-s1",
      "quote": "After this the customary offering to the dragon changed from human sacrifice to steamed rice with red beans.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Persembahan dalam legenda tersebut berubah menjadi nasi dengan kacang merah.",
        "en": "Offerings in that legend change to rice cooked with red beans."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kuzuryu-c07",
      "source_id": "kuzuryu-s2",
      "quote": "Like most dragons, kuzuryū have power over rain and water.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah belakangan memberi para naga kuasa atas hujan dan air.",
        "en": "A later account assigns the dragons power over rain and water."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kuzuryu-c08",
      "source_id": "kuzuryu-s2",
      "quote": "Their details vary from place to place and legend to legend.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Uraian belakangan menekankan perbedaan antartadisi setempat.",
        "en": "The later account stresses differences among local traditions."
      }
    }
  ],
```

```json
  "countries": null,

  "habitats": [],
  "disposition": null,
  "traits": [],
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Kyōrinrin adalah yōkai sutra dalam kumpulan ilustrasi Sekien.",
    "en": "Kyōrinrin is a sutra yōkai in Sekien’s illustrated collection.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "kyorinrin-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Kyōrinrin adalah yōkai sutra dalam kumpulan ilustrasi Sekien. Ciptaan Sekien mengacu pada adu kesaktian antara Shubin dan Kūkai. Sekien menduga sutra Shubin yang dibuang menjadi makhluk itu.",
      "en": "Kyōrinrin is a sutra yōkai in Sekien’s illustrated collection. Sekien’s invention draws on the magical contest between Shubin and Kūkai. Sekien speculates that Shubin’s discarded sutras became the creature.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "kyorinrin-c01",
        "kyorinrin-c02",
        "kyorinrin-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Desainnya menyerupai sosok berparuh panjang berbalut kitab dalam gulungan yang lebih tua. Penggambaran belakangan memberinya penutup kepala gulungan, paruh, dan lengan yang memanjang. Kisah belakangan itu menyebutnya menyerang pemilik yang mengabaikan buku.",
      "en": "Its design resembles a long-beaked figure wrapped in scriptures in an older scroll. A later depiction gives it a scroll headpiece, a beak and extendible arms. That later account has it attack owners who neglect the books.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "kyorinrin-c04",
        "kyorinrin-c05",
        "kyorinrin-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,
  "story_mode": null,
  "did_you_know": null,
  "abilities": [],
  "weaknesses": [],
  "variants": [],
  "stories": [],
  "places": [],

  "learning_questions": [],
  "images": [],
  "sources": [
    {
      "id": "kyorinrin-s1",
      "published": null,
      "accessed": "2026-10-01",
      "url": "https://ja.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E7%B5%8C%E5%87%9B%E3%80%85",
      "title": "経凛々",
      "publisher": "Wikipedia",
      "language": "ja",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "author": null
    },
    {
      "id": "kyorinrin-s2",
      "published": null,
      "accessed": "2026-10-01",
      "url": "https://yokai.com/kyourinrin/",
      "title": "Kyōrinrin",
      "publisher": "Yokai.com",
      "language": "en",
      "type": "encyclopedia",
      "author": "Matthew Meyer"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "kyorinrin-c01",
      "source_id": "kyorinrin-s1",
      "quote": "経凛々（きょうりんりん）は、鳥山石燕の妖怪画集『百器徒然袋』にある日本の妖怪の一つで、経文の妖怪。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kyōrinrin adalah yōkai sutra dalam kumpulan ilustrasi Sekien.",
        "en": "Kyōrinrin is a sutra yōkai in Sekien’s illustrated collection."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kyorinrin-c02",
      "source_id": "kyorinrin-s1",
      "quote": "軍記物語『太平記』にある平安時代前期の京都西寺の祈祷僧・守敏（しゅびん）が、東寺の僧・空海との法力比べで敗れ去った逸話をもとに石燕が創作した妖怪とされる。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ciptaan Sekien mengacu pada adu kesaktian antara Shubin dan Kūkai.",
        "en": "Sekien’s invention draws on the magical contest between Shubin and Kūkai."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kyorinrin-c03",
      "source_id": "kyorinrin-s1",
      "quote": "不用となって捨てられた守敏の経文が経凛々になったものだろうか、と石燕は解説文を書いている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sekien menduga sutra Shubin yang dibuang menjadi makhluk itu.",
        "en": "Sekien speculates that Shubin’s discarded sutras became the creature."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kyorinrin-c04",
      "source_id": "kyorinrin-s1",
      "quote": "『百器徒然袋』の妖怪は、室町時代の妖怪絵巻『百鬼夜行絵巻』をモチーフとして描かれたものが多いが、この経凛々についても同様に『百鬼夜行絵巻』の中にある、くちばしの長い鳥状の妖怪が経文を頭に巻いている姿にデザイン上の共通点が見られる。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Desainnya menyerupai sosok berparuh panjang berbalut kitab dalam gulungan yang lebih tua.",
        "en": "Its design resembles a long-beaked figure wrapped in scriptures in an older scroll."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kyorinrin-c05",
      "source_id": "kyorinrin-s2",
      "quote": "A scroll with tassels becomes the headpiece, and they develop bird-like beaks and long, extendible arms.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Penggambaran belakangan memberinya penutup kepala gulungan, paruh, dan lengan yang memanjang.",
        "en": "A later depiction gives it a scroll headpiece, a beak and extendible arms."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kyorinrin-c06",
      "source_id": "kyorinrin-s2",
      "quote": "The kyōrinrin use their elongating arms to assault the ignorant owners who let such priceless treasures and knowledge fall into disuse.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah belakangan itu menyebutnya menyerang pemilik yang mengabaikan buku.",
        "en": "That later account has it attack owners who neglect the books."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
  "gaps": [
    {
      "field": "images",
      "searched": "Belum ada berkas Wikimedia Commons yang diperiksa untuk entri ini."

}
```

```json
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Kyūso adalah tikus yang menjadi yōkai karena usia.",
    "en": "Kyūso is a rat that becomes a yōkai with age.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "kyuso-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Kyūso adalah tikus yang menjadi yōkai karena usia. Berbagai kisah menggambarkannya memakan kucing, membesarkan anak kucing, atau menyakiti manusia. Satu kisah menyebutnya membesarkan anak kucing yatim sebelum menghilang.",
      "en": "Kyūso is a rat that becomes a yōkai with age. Accounts variously describe it eating cats, raising kittens or harming humans. One story has it raise orphaned kittens before disappearing.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "kyuso-c01",
        "kyuso-c02",
        "kyuso-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Kisah lain menggambarkan tikus tiga warna di Yamato yang memakan kucing. Kisah belakangan membandingkan ukurannya dengan kucing atau anjing sedang. Uraian belakangan menduga permainan kata dari pepatah tikus terpojok menggigit kucing.",
      "en": "Another account describes a tricolored rat in Yamato that ate cats. A later account compares its size with cats or medium-sized dogs. The later account proposes a pun on the saying that a cornered rat bites a cat.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "kyuso-c04",
        "kyuso-c05",
        "kyuso-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,
  "story_mode": null,
  "did_you_know": null,
  "abilities": [],
  "weaknesses": [],
  "variants": [],
  "stories": [],

      "id": "kyuso-s1",

      "url": "https://ja.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E6%97%A7%E9%BC%A0",
      "title": "旧鼠",

    {
      "id": "kyuso-s2",
      "published": null,

      "url": "https://yokai.com/kyuuso/",
      "title": "Kyūso",
      "publisher": "Yokai.com",
      "language": "en",
      "type": "encyclopedia",
      "author": "Matthew Meyer"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "kyuso-c01",
      "source_id": "kyuso-s1",
      "quote": "旧鼠（きゅうそ）は、日本の妖怪の一つで、ネズミが歳月を経て妖怪となったもの。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kyūso adalah tikus yang menjadi yōkai karena usia.",
        "en": "Kyūso is a rat that becomes a yōkai with age."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kyuso-c02",
      "source_id": "kyuso-s1",
      "quote": "『絵本百物語』『翁草』（おきなぐさ）などの江戸時代の古書や民間伝承にあるもので、ネコすらも食べるもの、子猫を育てるもの、人間に害をなすものなどがいたとされる。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Berbagai kisah menggambarkannya memakan kucing, membesarkan anak kucing, atau menyakiti manusia.",
        "en": "Accounts variously describe it eating cats, raising kittens or harming humans."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kyuso-c03",
      "source_id": "kyuso-s1",
      "quote": "親無しとなった子ネコたちに対して旧鼠は、夜な夜なそのもとへやってきてこれらの世話をし、ネコたちが無事に育った後にどこかへと姿を消した。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Satu kisah menyebutnya membesarkan anak kucing yatim sebelum menghilang.",
        "en": "One story has it raise orphaned kittens before disappearing."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kyuso-c04",
      "source_id": "kyuso-s1",
      "quote": "また同書の挿絵中にある文章によれば、大和国（現在の奈良県）にいた旧鼠は、その毛色が赤白黒の三毛のもので、いつもネコを食べていたという。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah lain menggambarkan tikus tiga warna di Yamato yang memakan kucing.",
        "en": "Another account describes a tricolored rat in Yamato that ate cats."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kyuso-c05",
      "source_id": "kyuso-s2",
      "quote": "They look like ordinary rats, only they are as large as cats or even medium-sized dogs.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah belakangan membandingkan ukurannya dengan kucing atau anjing sedang.",
        "en": "A later account compares its size with cats or medium-sized dogs."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kyuso-c06",
      "source_id": "kyuso-s2",
      "quote": "Its name is likely a pun. There is an old saying: “ kyūso neko wo kamu ” (“a cornered rat will bite a cat”).",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Uraian belakangan menduga permainan kata dari pepatah tikus terpojok menggigit kucing.",
        "en": "The later account proposes a pun on the saying that a cornered rat bites a cat."
      }
    }
  ],
```

```json
  "claims": [

      "id": "mamedanuki-c01",
      "source_id": "mamedanuki-s1",
      "quote": "豆狸（まめだぬき）は、日本に伝わるタヌキの妖怪。西日本に伝承されているほか、江戸時代の奇談集『絵本百物語』にも記述がある。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mamedanuki adalah yōkai tanuki dalam tradisi Jepang barat dan sastra Edo.",
        "en": "Mamedanuki is a tanuki yōkai in western Japanese tradition and Edo literature."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mamedanuki-c02",
      "source_id": "mamedanuki-s1",
      "quote": "山陽地方の豆狸 山陽地方では豆狸をマメダと呼び、山村の旧家の納戸にいるもので、ときおり3,4歳ほどの子供くらいの大きさの老婆の姿に化け、納戸に無言で座っていることがあるという。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Versi Sanyō berubah menjadi perempuan tua kecil yang duduk diam di gudang.",
        "en": "A Sanyō version becomes a tiny old woman sitting silently in a storeroom."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mamedanuki-c03",
      "source_id": "mamedanuki-s1",
      "quote": "この地の豆狸はこうした悪戯を好むにもかかわらず、酒造の業者には崇められ、蔵に豆狸が1,2匹はいないと良い酒ができないと言われていた。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pembuat sake Nada memuja si pengusil dan menghubungkannya dengan sake yang baik.",
        "en": "Nada brewers revere the prankster and link it with good sake."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mamedanuki-c04",
      "source_id": "mamedanuki-s1",
      "quote": "徳島県の豆狸 徳島県の豆狸は夜になると山頂に火を灯したといい、それは次の日に必ず雨が降る知らせだったという。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",

        "id": "Versi Tokushima menyalakan api di puncak gunung sebagai pertanda hujan.",
```

```json
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "many-finned-sea-serpent-c01",
      "source_id": "many-finned-sea-serpent-s1",
      "quote": "ムカデクジラ（英: Many-finned sea serpent）は、側面や背中に多数のヒレがあるとされている海棲未確認動物。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Many-finned sea serpent adalah kriptid laut yang dikabarkan memiliki banyak sirip.",
        "en": "The many-finned sea serpent is an alleged marine cryptid with numerous fins."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "many-finned-sea-serpent-c02",
      "source_id": "many-finned-sea-serpent-s1",
      "quote": "古代ギリシアの著述家アイリアノスは『動物の性質について』（後2世紀）において、海岸でこのような動物（ギリシア語でスコロペンドラ, Σκολοπενδρα）が知られていると報告している。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Aelian mencatat makhluk pesisir sebanding yang disebut scolopendra.",
        "en": "Aelian reports a comparable coastal creature called scolopendra."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "many-finned-sea-serpent-c03",
      "source_id": "many-finned-sea-serpent-s1",
      "quote": "アイリアノスはさらに、この動物にはロブスターのような尾と毛のある大きな鼻があるとする目撃証言を伝えている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Catatan kuno itu memberinya ekor menyerupai lobster dan hidung besar berbulu.",
        "en": "That ancient report gives it a lobster-like tail and a large hairy nose."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "many-finned-sea-serpent-c04",
      "source_id": "many-finned-sea-serpent-s1",
      "quote": "動物学者のベルナール・ユーヴェルマンはこの未確認動物を「多鰭型」のシーサーペントであると分類して、仮に学名ケティオスコロペンドラ・アエリアナ（Cetioscolopendra aeliana）を与えた。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bernard Heuvelmans menggolongkannya sebagai ular laut bersirip banyak.",
        "en": "Bernard Heuvelmans classifies it as a many-finned sea serpent."
      }
    }
  ],
```

```json
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Nyakuichi Ōji adalah dewa sinkretis Shinto-Buddha yang juga disebut Nyakuōji. Ia menempati urutan pertama dalam lima Ōji dari dua belas gongen Kumano.",
      "en": "Nyakuichi Ōji is a syncretic Shinto-Buddhist deity also called Nyakuōji. He ranks first among the five Ōji of Kumano’s twelve gongen.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "nyakuichi-oji-c01",
        "nyakuichi-oji-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Padanan Buddhanya adalah Kannon berwajah sebelas, sedangkan penyamaan mencakup Amaterasu atau Ninigi. Pemisahan Buddha dan Shinto pada zaman Meiji mengganti sebagian nama kuil, sementara lainnya tetap memakai namanya.",
      "en": "His Buddhist counterpart is eleven-faced Kannon, while identifications include Amaterasu or Ninigi. Meiji separation of Buddhism and Shinto changed some shrine names while others retained his name.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "nyakuichi-oji-c03",
        "nyakuichi-oji-c04"
      ]
    }

  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "nyakuichi-oji-c01",
      "source_id": "nyakuichi-oji-s1",
      "quote": "若一王子（にゃくいちおうじ）は、神仏習合の神である。若王子（にゃくおうじ）ともいう。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nyakuichi Ōji adalah dewa sinkretis Shinto-Buddha yang juga disebut Nyakuōji.",
        "en": "Nyakuichi Ōji is a syncretic Shinto-Buddhist deity also called Nyakuōji."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nyakuichi-oji-c02",
      "source_id": "nyakuichi-oji-s1",
      "quote": "熊野三山に祀られる熊野十二所権現は三所権現・五所王子・四所明神に分けられ、若一王子は五所王子の第一位である。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia menempati urutan pertama dalam lima Ōji dari dua belas gongen Kumano.",
        "en": "He ranks first among the five Ōji of Kumano’s twelve gongen."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nyakuichi-oji-c03",
      "source_id": "nyakuichi-oji-s1",
      "quote": "若一王子の本地仏は十一面観音で、天照大神あるいは瓊々杵尊と同一視された。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Padanan Buddhanya adalah Kannon berwajah sebelas, sedangkan penyamaan mencakup Amaterasu atau Ninigi.",
        "en": "His Buddhist counterpart is eleven-faced Kannon, while identifications include Amaterasu or Ninigi."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nyakuichi-oji-c04",
      "source_id": "nyakuichi-oji-s1",
      "quote": "明治の神仏分離に伴い、「若一王子」を天照大神や瓊々杵尊に変えた所も多いが、従前のまま「若一王子」として祀っている神社もある。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pemisahan Buddha dan Shinto pada zaman Meiji mengganti sebagian nama kuil, sementara lainnya tetap memakai namanya.",
        "en": "Meiji separation of Buddhism and Shinto changed some shrine names while others retained his name."
      }
```

```json
  },
  "countries": null,

  "habitats": [],

  "traits": [],
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Nyūdō-bōzu adalah yōkai yang dikisahkan di Aichi dan prefektur Jepang lainnya.",
    "en": "Nyūdō-bōzu is a yōkai reported in Aichi and other Japanese prefectures.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "nyudo-bozu-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Nyūdō-bōzu adalah yōkai yang dikisahkan di Aichi dan prefektur Jepang lainnya. Ia bertambah tinggi ketika pengamat menengadah semakin jauh. Versi Aichi tumbuh dari biksu kecil menjadi laki-laki besar.",
      "en": "Nyūdō-bōzu is a yōkai reported in Aichi and other Japanese prefectures. It grows taller the further an observer looks upward. The Aichi version grows from a small monk into a large man.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "nyudo-bozu-c01",
        "nyudo-bozu-c02",
        "nyudo-bozu-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Satu ucapan dipercaya membuatnya menghilang, sedangkan balasannya dapat mendatangkan kematian. Tradisi Fukushima mengidentifikasinya sebagai cerpelai. Daerah lain mengidentifikasi wujud aslinya sebagai tanuki atau mujina.",
      "en": "A saying is believed to make it disappear, while its reply can bring death. A Fukushima tradition identifies it as a weasel. Other regions identify its true form as a tanuki or mujina.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "nyudo-bozu-c04",
        "nyudo-bozu-c05",
        "nyudo-bozu-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,
  "story_mode": null,
  "did_you_know": null,
  "abilities": [],
  "weaknesses": [],
  "variants": [],
  "stories": [],
  "places": [],
  "timeline": [],
  "relations": [],
  "modern_depictions": [],
  "tradition_vs_modern": null,
  "learning_questions": [],
  "images": [],
  "sources": [
    {
      "id": "nyudo-bozu-s1",
      "published": null,
      "accessed": "2026-10-01",
      "url": "https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ny%C5%ABd%C5%8D-b%C5%8Dzu",
      "title": "Nyūdō-bōzu",
      "publisher": "Wikipedia",
      "language": "en",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "author": null
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "nyudo-bozu-c01",
      "source_id": "nyudo-bozu-s1",
      "quote": "Nyūdō-bōzu (Japanese: 入道坊主) are yōkai first known in Tsukude, Minamishitara District, Aichi Prefecture (now Shinshiro), and then also in Fukushima Prefecture; Kurikoma, Kurihara District, Miyagi Prefecture (now Kurihara); Hirano, Suwa District, Nagano Prefecture (now Okaya), among other places.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nyūdō-bōzu adalah yōkai yang dikisahkan di Aichi dan prefektur Jepang lainnya.",
        "en": "Nyūdō-bōzu is a yōkai reported in Aichi and other Japanese prefectures."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nyudo-bozu-c02",
      "source_id": "nyudo-bozu-s1",
      "quote": "It is a yōkai that becomes larger the further one looks up, and is thus considered a type of mikoshi-nyūdō, which has the same characteristics.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia bertambah tinggi ketika pengamat menengadah semakin jauh.",
        "en": "It grows taller the further an observer looks upward."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nyudo-bozu-c03",
      "source_id": "nyudo-bozu-s1",
      "quote": "In Aichi Prefecture, it appears as a small bōzu that has a height that is not even 3 shaku (about 1 meter), but when people who meet them get close to it, it would then increase in height and become a large man about 7 or 8 shaku to 1 to (about 2 to 3 meters).",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Versi Aichi tumbuh dari biksu kecil menjadi laki-laki besar.",
        "en": "The Aichi version grows from a small monk into a large man."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nyudo-bozu-c04",
      "source_id": "nyudo-bozu-s1",
      "quote": "It is said that by saying to it \"I've seen it (見ていたぞ),\" it is possible to make it disappear, but in reverse if it says something instead, it results in death.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Satu ucapan dipercaya membuatnya menghilang, sedangkan balasannya dapat mendatangkan kematian.",
        "en": "A saying is believed to make it disappear, while its reply can bring death."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nyudo-bozu-c05",
      "source_id": "nyudo-bozu-s1",
      "quote": "In Fukushima Prefecture, it is said to be a weasel.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tradisi Fukushima mengidentifikasinya sebagai cerpelai.",
        "en": "A Fukushima tradition identifies it as a weasel."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nyudo-bozu-c06",
      "source_id": "nyudo-bozu-s1",
      "quote": "In Nagano Prefecture, it is said that their true form is considered to be a tanuki or mujina, and in Miyagi Prefecture, a mujina.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Daerah lain mengidentifikasi wujud aslinya sebagai tanuki atau mujina.",
        "en": "Other regions identify its true form as a tanuki or mujina."
      }
    }
  ],
```

```json
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Obariyon adalah yōkai Sanjō di Niigata dengan beberapa nama daerah.",
    "en": "Obariyon is a yōkai of Sanjō in Niigata with several regional names.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "obariyon-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Obariyon adalah yōkai Sanjō di Niigata dengan beberapa nama daerah. Ia melompat ke punggung pelintas pada malam hari. Beban itu semakin berat dan sulit dilepaskan.",
      "en": "Obariyon is a yōkai of Sanjō in Niigata with several regional names. It jumps onto a traveler’s back at night. The burden becomes increasingly heavy and difficult to remove.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "obariyon-c01",
        "obariyon-c02",
        "obariyon-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Satu dongeng memberi pembawanya ganjaran emas ketika pulang. Namanya dijelaskan sebagai permintaan gendongan dalam dialek Niigata. Kisah belakangan menyebutnya menggigit kulit kepala pembawanya.",
      "en": "One folktale rewards the carrier with gold at home. Its name is explained as a Niigata request for a piggyback ride. A later account says it chews the carrier’s scalp.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "obariyon-c04",
        "obariyon-c05",
        "obariyon-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,
  "story_mode": null,
  "did_you_know": null,
  "abilities": [],
  "weaknesses": [],
  "variants": [],
  "stories": [],
  "places": [],
  "timeline": [],
  "relations": [],
  "modern_depictions": [],
  "tradition_vs_modern": null,

  "sources": [
    {
      "id": "obariyon-s1",
      "published": null,
      "accessed": "2026-10-01",
      "url": "https://ja.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E3%81%8A%E3%81%B0%E3%82%8A%E3%82%88%E3%82%93",
      "title": "おばりよん",
      "publisher": "Wikipedia",
      "language": "ja",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "author": null
    },
    {
      "id": "obariyon-s2",
      "published": null,
      "accessed": "2026-10-01",
      "url": "https://yokai.com/obariyon/",
      "title": "Obariyon",
      "publisher": "Yokai.com",
      "language": "en",
      "type": "encyclopedia",
      "author": "Matthew Meyer"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "obariyon-c01",
      "source_id": "obariyon-s1",
      "quote": "おばりよんは、新潟県三条市に伝わる妖怪の一種。大正時代の新潟の民俗誌『越後三条南郷談』には「ばりよん」の名で記載があり、他にも「おんぶおばけ」「うばりよん」「おぼさりてい」とも言われる。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Obariyon adalah yōkai Sanjō di Niigata dengan beberapa nama daerah.",
        "en": "Obariyon is a yōkai of Sanjō in Niigata with several regional names."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "obariyon-c02",
      "source_id": "obariyon-s1",
      "quote": "夜、藪の中を歩いていると、いきなり「おばりよん」と叫びながら背中におぶさってくる。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia melompat ke punggung pelintas pada malam hari.",
        "en": "It jumps onto a traveler’s back at night."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "obariyon-c03",
      "source_id": "obariyon-s1",
      "quote": "そしてその体の小ささに似合わずどんどん重くなっていき、簡単に離れなくなるとされる。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Beban itu semakin berat dan sulit dilepaskan.",
        "en": "The burden becomes increasingly heavy and difficult to remove."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "obariyon-c04",
      "source_id": "obariyon-s1",
      "quote": "ある者が化物の現れるという場所に出かけたところ、「ばろんばろん」と声がするので「負（ば）れたかったら負れろ」と言うと、重たい何者かが背に乗り、仕方なく背負ったまま帰宅すると、それが大量の金になっていたという話である。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Satu dongeng memberi pembawanya ganjaran emas ketika pulang.",
        "en": "One folktale rewards the carrier with gold at home."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "obariyon-c05",
      "source_id": "obariyon-s2",
      "quote": "The name obariyon comes from a phrase in local Niigata dialect meaning “give me a piggyback ride!”",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Namanya dijelaskan sebagai permintaan gendongan dalam dialek Niigata.",
        "en": "Its name is explained as a Niigata request for a piggyback ride."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "obariyon-c06",
      "source_id": "obariyon-s2",
      "quote": "this mischievous yokai chews the scalp of a person good enough to carry it",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah belakangan menyebutnya menggigit kulit kepala pembawanya.",
        "en": "A later account says it chews the carrier’s scalp."
      }
    }
  ],
```

```json
  "short_description": {
```
