    "en": "Ogetsuhime is a food goddess in Japanese mythology and the Kojiki.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "ogetsuhime-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Ogetsuhime adalah dewi makanan dalam mitologi Jepang dan Kojiki. Namanya berarti dewi makanan agung. Susanoo melihatnya menyiapkan makanan yang diambil dari tubuhnya.",
      "en": "Ogetsuhime is a food goddess in Japanese mythology and the Kojiki. Her name means great food goddess. Susanoo sees her prepare food taken from her body.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "ogetsuhime-c01",
        "ogetsuhime-c02",
        "ogetsuhime-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Susanoo membunuhnya karena marah pada asal makanan tersebut. Ulat sutra dan beberapa tanaman muncul dari bagian tubuhnya yang mati. Legenda Iwami menyebut Otogosahime sebagai putrinya yang membawa benih ke bumi.",
      "en": "Susanoo kills her in anger at the source of the food. Silkworms and several crops emerge from parts of her dead body. An Iwami legend names Otogosahime as her daughter who brings seeds to earth.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "ogetsuhime-c04",
        "ogetsuhime-c05",
        "ogetsuhime-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,
  "story_mode": null,
  "did_you_know": null,
  "abilities": [],

  "sources": [
    {
      "id": "ogetsuhime-s1",
      "published": null,
      "accessed": "2026-10-01",
      "url": "https://ja.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E3%82%AA%E3%82%AA%E3%82%B2%E3%83%84%E3%83%92%E3%83%A1",
      "title": "オオゲツヒメ",
      "publisher": "Wikipedia",
      "language": "ja",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "author": null
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "ogetsuhime-c01",
      "source_id": "ogetsuhime-s1",
      "quote": "オオゲツヒメ（オホゲツヒメ、オオゲツヒメノカミ、大宜都比売、大気都比売神、大宜津比売神、大気津比売神）は、日本神話に登場する女神。『古事記』の神話に出る食物の女神。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ogetsuhime adalah dewi makanan dalam mitologi Jepang dan Kojiki.",
        "en": "Ogetsuhime is a food goddess in Japanese mythology and the Kojiki."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ogetsuhime-c02",
      "source_id": "ogetsuhime-s1",
      "quote": "オオゲツヒメという名称は「大いなる食物の女神」の意味である。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Namanya berarti dewi makanan agung.",
        "en": "Her name means great food goddess."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ogetsuhime-c03",
      "source_id": "ogetsuhime-s1",
      "quote": "様々な食べ物を差し出してきた大気都比売神を不審に思った須佐之男命が食事の用意をする大気都比売神の様子を覗いてみると、大気都比売神は鼻や口、尻から食材を取り出し、それを調理していた。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Susanoo melihatnya menyiapkan makanan yang diambil dari tubuhnya.",
        "en": "Susanoo sees her prepare food taken from her body."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ogetsuhime-c04",
      "source_id": "ogetsuhime-s1",
      "quote": "須佐之男命は、そんな汚い物を食べさせようとしていたのかと怒り、大気都比売神を殺してしまった。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Susanoo membunuhnya karena marah pada asal makanan tersebut.",
        "en": "Susanoo kills her in anger at the source of the food."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ogetsuhime-c05",
      "source_id": "ogetsuhime-s1",
      "quote": "すると、大気都比売神の頭から蚕が生まれ、目から稲が生まれ、耳から粟が生まれ、鼻から小豆が生まれ、陰部から麦が生まれ、尻から大豆が生まれた。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ulat sutra dan beberapa tanaman muncul dari bagian tubuhnya yang mati.",
        "en": "Silkworms and several crops emerge from parts of her dead body."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ogetsuhime-c06",
      "source_id": "ogetsuhime-s1",
      "quote": "また島根県石見地方に伝わる伝説には、大気都比売神の娘に乙子狭姫がおり、雁に乗って降臨し作物の種を地上に伝えたとする。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Legenda Iwami menyebut Otogosahime sebagai putrinya yang membawa benih ke bumi.",
        "en": "An Iwami legend names Otogosahime as her daughter who brings seeds to earth."
      }
    }
  ],

      "source_id": "omonoimi-no-kami-s1",
      "quote": "大物忌神（おおものいみのかみ）は、山形県の鳥海山に宿るとされる神である。鳥海山頂の鳥海山大物忌神社をはじめ、東北地方各地に大物忌神を祀る神社がある。",

      "id": "omonoimi-no-kami-c02",
      "source_id": "omonoimi-no-kami-s1",
      "quote": "鳥海山は古代のヤマト王権の支配圏の北辺にあることから、大物忌神は国家を守る神とされ、また、穢れを清める神ともされた。",

      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {

        "en": "He is associated with protection of the state and purification."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "omonoimi-no-kami-c03",
      "source_id": "omonoimi-no-kami-s1",
      "quote": "鳥海山は火山であり、鳥海山の噴火は大物忌神の怒りであると考えられ、噴火のたびにより高い神階が授けられた。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Letusan gunung ditafsirkan sebagai kemarahannya.",
        "en": "Volcanic eruptions were interpreted as his anger."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "omonoimi-no-kami-c04",
      "source_id": "omonoimi-no-kami-s1",
      "quote": "大物忌神は、倉稲魂命・豊受大神・大忌神・広瀬神などと同神とされる。鳥海山大物忌神社の社伝では神宮外宮の豊受大神と同神としている。",
      "locator": "Article text",

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Onmoraki adalah burung ganjil dalam kitab lama Tiongkok dan Jepang.",
    "en": "Onmoraki is a strange bird in Chinese and Japanese old books.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "onmoraki-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Onmoraki adalah burung ganjil dalam kitab lama Tiongkok dan Jepang. Kisah menghubungkannya dengan jenazah baru yang kekurangan upacara semestinya. Kisah Sekien memberinya tubuh hitam mirip bangau, mata bercahaya, dan suara melengking.",
      "en": "Onmoraki is a strange bird in Chinese and Japanese old books. Accounts connect it with recent corpses lacking proper rites. Sekien’s account gives it a black crane-like body, shining eyes and a shrill voice.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "onmoraki-c01",
        "onmoraki-c02",
        "onmoraki-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Buku zaman Edo memuat kisah Jepang yang menyerupai kisah Tiongkok. Kisah belakangan menyebut mereka memarahi pendeta yang tidur dengan meniru suaranya. Dalam kisah itu ia menghilang ketika pendeta melarikan diri.",
      "en": "An Edo book contains a Japanese story resembling the Chinese account. A later account says they scold sleeping priests in an imitation of their voices. In that account it vanishes when the priest flees.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "onmoraki-c04",
        "onmoraki-c05",
        "onmoraki-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,
  "story_mode": null,
  "did_you_know": null,
  "abilities": [],
  "weaknesses": [],
  "variants": [],

    {
      "id": "onmoraki-s1",

      "publisher": "Wikipedia",
      "language": "ja",

      "author": null
    },
    {
      "id": "onmoraki-s2",
      "published": null,
      "accessed": "2026-10-01",
      "url": "https://yokai.com/onmoraki/",
      "title": "Onmoraki",
      "publisher": "Yokai.com",
      "language": "en",
      "type": "encyclopedia",
      "author": "Matthew Meyer"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "onmoraki-c01",
      "source_id": "onmoraki-s1",
      "quote": "陰摩羅鬼（おんもらき、陰魔羅鬼）は、中国や日本の古書にある怪鳥。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Onmoraki adalah burung ganjil dalam kitab lama Tiongkok dan Jepang.",
        "en": "Onmoraki is a strange bird in Chinese and Japanese old books."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "onmoraki-c02",
      "source_id": "onmoraki-s1",
      "quote": "経典『大蔵経』によれば、新しい死体から生じた気が化けたものとされる。充分な供養を受けていない死体が化けたもので、経文読みを怠っている僧侶のもとに現れるともいう。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah menghubungkannya dengan jenazah baru yang kekurangan upacara semestinya.",
        "en": "Accounts connect it with recent corpses lacking proper rites."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "onmoraki-c03",
      "source_id": "onmoraki-s1",
      "quote": "古典の画図においては鳥山石燕の画集『今昔画図続百鬼』に描かれており、解説文には中国の古書『清尊録』からの引用で、姿は鶴のようで、体色が黒く、眼光は灯火のようで、羽を震わせて甲高く鳴くとある。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah Sekien memberinya tubuh hitam mirip bangau, mata bercahaya, dan suara melengking.",
        "en": "Sekien’s account gives it a black crane-like body, shining eyes and a shrill voice."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "onmoraki-c04",
      "source_id": "onmoraki-s1",
      "quote": "日本では江戸時代の書物『太平百物語』に、『清尊録』に類似した陰摩羅鬼の話がある。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Buku zaman Edo memuat kisah Jepang yang menyerupai kisah Tiongkok.",
        "en": "An Edo book contains a Japanese story resembling the Chinese account."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "onmoraki-c05",
      "source_id": "onmoraki-s2",
      "quote": "They sneak up on sleeping priests and surprise them, scolding them in a perfect imitation of their own voices.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah belakangan menyebut mereka memarahi pendeta yang tidur dengan meniru suaranya.",
        "en": "A later account says they scold sleeping priests in an imitation of their voices."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "onmoraki-c06",
      "source_id": "onmoraki-s2",
      "quote": "When the priest wakes up and flees in terror, the onmoraki vanishes into the shadows.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam kisah itu ia menghilang ketika pendeta melarikan diri.",
        "en": "In that account it vanishes when the priest flees."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
  "gaps": [
    {
      "field": "images",
      "searched": "Belum ada berkas Wikimedia Commons yang diperiksa untuk entri ini."

  ]
}

    "claim_ids": [

    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Ōnyūdō adalah yōkai gaib dalam cerita rakyat Jepang. Namanya berarti biksu besar, meskipun wujud daerahnya beragam. Versi menyerupai biksu juga disebut Ōbōzu.",
      "en": "Ōnyūdō is a supernatural yōkai in Japanese folklore. Its name means big monk, although regional forms vary. Monk-like versions are also called Ōbōzu.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "onyudo-c01",
        "onyudo-c02",
        "onyudo-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ukuran yang dikisahkan berkisar dari dua meter sampai raksasa sebesar gunung. Sebagian kisah mengaitkan melihatnya dengan penyakit. Sebagian versi mengidentifikasinya sebagai rubah, tanuki, atau pagoda batu yang berubah.",
      "en": "Reported sizes range from two meters to mountain-sized giants. Some accounts associate seeing it with illness. Some versions identify it as transformed foxes, tanuki or stone pagodas.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "onyudo-c04",
        "onyudo-c05",
        "onyudo-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,
  "story_mode": null,

      "id": "onyudo-s1",
      "published": null,

  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "onyudo-c01",
      "source_id": "onyudo-s1",
      "quote": "Ōnyūdō is a type of yōkai, or supernatural creature, found in Japanese folklore.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ōnyūdō adalah yōkai gaib dalam cerita rakyat Jepang.",
        "en": "Ōnyūdō is a supernatural yōkai in Japanese folklore."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "onyudo-c02",
      "source_id": "onyudo-s1",
      "quote": "The name Ōnyūdō means \"big monk.\" However, depending on the region, legends vary: in some accounts, their appearance is described as a vague shadow or giant than a monk.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Namanya berarti biksu besar, meskipun wujud daerahnya beragam.",
        "en": "Its name means big monk, although regional forms vary."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "onyudo-c03",
      "source_id": "onyudo-s1",
      "quote": "Those depicted as resembling a monk (bōzu) are also referred to as Ōbōzu.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Versi menyerupai biksu juga disebut Ōbōzu.",
        "en": "Monk-like versions are also called Ōbōzu."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "onyudo-c04",
      "source_id": "onyudo-s1",
      "quote": "Their reported size ranges from about 2 meters in height to giants as large as mountains.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ukuran yang dikisahkan berkisar dari dua meter sampai raksasa sebesar gunung.",
        "en": "Reported sizes range from two meters to mountain-sized giants."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "onyudo-c05",
      "source_id": "onyudo-s1",
      "quote": "Many legends portray Ōnyūdō as menacing figures, with stories claiming that those who see one may fall ill.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebagian kisah mengaitkan melihatnya dengan penyakit.",
        "en": "Some accounts associate seeing it with illness."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "onyudo-c06",
      "source_id": "onyudo-s1",
      "quote": "In some tales, they are said to be foxes (kitsune) or tanuki in disguise, or even stone pagodas that have transformed into living beings.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebagian versi mengidentifikasinya sebagai rubah, tanuki, atau pagoda batu yang berubah.",
        "en": "Some versions identify it as transformed foxes, tanuki or stone pagodas."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {

    "claim_ids": [

    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Ōtakemaru adalah sosok gaib dalam cerita rakyat Jepang. Tempat tinggalnya adalah Pegunungan Suzuka antara Ise dan Ōmi. Lawan legendanya adalah Sakanoue no Tamuramaro pada masa Kanmu.",
      "en": "Ōtakemaru is a supernatural figure in Japanese folklore. His dwelling is the Suzuka Mountains between Ise and Ōmi. His legendary opponent is Sakanoue no Tamuramaro in Kanmu’s reign.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "ootakemaru-c01",
        "ootakemaru-c02",
        "ootakemaru-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia dikisahkan mengendalikan badai, petir, dan awan yang menghujankan api. Kisah belakangan menyebutnya kijin, sekaligus iblis dan dewa. Uraian belakangan mencatat bahwa sebagian peringkat menggantikannya dengan Sutoku Tennō.",
      "en": "He is credited with storms, thunder and clouds raining fire. A later account calls him a kijin, both demon and god. The later account notes that some rankings replace him with Sutoku Tennō.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "ootakemaru-c04",
        "ootakemaru-c05",
        "ootakemaru-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "ootakemaru-c01",
      "source_id": "ootakemaru-s1",
      "quote": "Ootakemaru (大嶽丸, also sometimes called 鬼神魔王、大だけ丸、or 大竹丸) is a figure in Japanese folklore.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ōtakemaru adalah sosok gaib dalam cerita rakyat Jepang.",
        "en": "Ōtakemaru is a supernatural figure in Japanese folklore."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ootakemaru-c02",
      "source_id": "ootakemaru-s1",
      "quote": "He resides on Suzuka Mountain, a connection of mountains and ravines bordering the Ise and Oumi countries.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tempat tinggalnya adalah Pegunungan Suzuka antara Ise dan Ōmi.",
        "en": "His dwelling is the Suzuka Mountains between Ise and Ōmi."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ootakemaru-c03",
      "source_id": "ootakemaru-s1",
      "quote": "He lived during the reign of Emperor Kanmu (781-806) and was one of the chief enemies of shogun Sakanoue no Tamuramaro.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Lawan legendanya adalah Sakanoue no Tamuramaro pada masa Kanmu.",
        "en": "His legendary opponent is Sakanoue no Tamuramaro in Kanmu’s reign."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ootakemaru-c04",
      "source_id": "ootakemaru-s1",
      "quote": "Ootakemaru was said to be able to manipulate the weather such as storms and thunder, capable of creating dark clouds to cover Suzuka Mountain that rained fire.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia dikisahkan mengendalikan badai, petir, dan awan yang menghujankan api.",
        "en": "He is credited with storms, thunder and clouds raining fire."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ootakemaru-c05",
      "source_id": "ootakemaru-s2",
      "quote": "Ōtakemaru is a kijin —an oni so powerful and so violent that he is considered both demon ( ki ) and god ( jin ).",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah belakangan menyebutnya kijin, sekaligus iblis dan dewa.",
        "en": "A later account calls him a kijin, both demon and god."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ootakemaru-c06",
      "source_id": "ootakemaru-s2",
      "quote": "Some versions of this ranking replace Ōtakemaru with Sutoku Tennō.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Uraian belakangan mencatat bahwa sebagian peringkat menggantikannya dengan Sutoku Tennō.",

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Oshiroibabā adalah yōkai perempuan tua dari daerah Totsukawa di Nara.",
    "en": "Oshiroibabā is an old-woman yōkai from the Totsukawa area of Nara.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "oshiroibaba-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Oshiroibabā adalah yōkai perempuan tua dari daerah Totsukawa di Nara. Ia menyeret cermin dengan suara gemerincing. Sekien menggambarkannya membungkuk dengan payung, tongkat, dan botol sake.",
      "en": "Oshiroibabā is an old-woman yōkai from the Totsukawa area of Nara. She drags a mirror with a jingling sound. Sekien depicts her bent-backed with an umbrella, cane and sake flask.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "oshiroibaba-c01",
        "oshiroibaba-c02",
        "oshiroibaba-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Sekien menyebutnya pembantu dewi kosmetik, tetapi hubungan dengan cerita Nara tidak pasti. Uraian belakangan menekankan riasan putih tebal yang berantakan. Uraian belakangan itu menyebutnya meminta riasan atau sake kepada orang.",
      "en": "Sekien calls her an attendant of a cosmetic goddess, but the relation to Nara folklore is uncertain. A later description emphasizes thick, messy white makeup. That later description has her ask people for makeup or sake.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "oshiroibaba-c04",
        "oshiroibaba-c05",
        "oshiroibaba-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],

    {
      "id": "oshiroibaba-s2",
      "published": null,

      "publisher": "Yokai.com",
      "language": "en",
      "type": "encyclopedia",
      "author": "Matthew Meyer"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "oshiroibaba-c01",
      "source_id": "oshiroibaba-s1",
      "quote": "Oshiroibabā (Japanese: 白粉ばばあ; lit. \"oshiroi (white facial powder) old woman\") or Oshiroibabā-san (白粉ばあさん) is a yōkai old woman from the Totsukawa river in Yoshino District, Nara Prefecture.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Oshiroibabā adalah yōkai perempuan tua dari daerah Totsukawa di Nara.",
        "en": "Oshiroibabā is an old-woman yōkai from the Totsukawa area of Nara."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "oshiroibaba-c02",
      "source_id": "oshiroibaba-s1",
      "quote": "The oshiroibabā is said to be a yōkai in the form of an old woman who appears while dragging a mirror and making a jingling sound.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia menyeret cermin dengan suara gemerincing.",
        "en": "She drags a mirror with a jingling sound."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "oshiroibaba-c03",
      "source_id": "oshiroibaba-s1",
      "quote": "Toriyama Sekien's Konjaku Hyakki Shūi depicts an old woman with a very bent back, wearing a large broken umbrella on her head, holding a walking stick with her right hand and a tokkuri (flask for sake) in her left hand, under the name of oshiroibabā.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sekien menggambarkannya membungkuk dengan payung, tongkat, dan botol sake.",
        "en": "Sekien depicts her bent-backed with an umbrella, cane and sake flask."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "oshiroibaba-c04",
      "source_id": "oshiroibaba-s1",
      "quote": "The commentary on the picture says, “the goddess of red and white oshiroi (facial powder) powder is called Shifunsenjo, and oshiroibabā must be an attendant of this goddess,” but it is unclear whether this is supposed to be the same oshiroibabā in Nara folklore.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sekien menyebutnya pembantu dewi kosmetik, tetapi hubungan dengan cerita Nara tidak pasti.",
        "en": "Sekien calls her an attendant of a cosmetic goddess, but the relation to Nara folklore is uncertain."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "oshiroibaba-c05",
      "source_id": "oshiroibaba-s2",
      "quote": "Their most defining feature is their wrinkly old face caked with thick, sloppy, white face powder.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Uraian belakangan menekankan riasan putih tebal yang berantakan.",
        "en": "A later description emphasizes thick, messy white makeup."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "oshiroibaba-c06",
      "source_id": "oshiroibaba-s2",
      "quote": "They occasionally accost people, demanding makeup or trying to buy sake.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Uraian belakangan itu menyebutnya meminta riasan atau sake kepada orang.",
        "en": "That later description has her ask people for makeup or sake."
      }
    }
  ],

    {
      "field": "images",

        "oyamakui-c02",
        "oyamakui-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Garis keturunannya ditafsirkan berkaitan dengan pertanian dan tanah. Ia dikaitkan dengan gunung dan kesehatan yang baik. Ia dipuja di Hiyoshi Taisha dan kuil terkait.",

      "id": "oyamakui-c02",
      "source_id": "oyamakui-s1",
      "quote": "The Kojiki says they live on Mount Hiei in Ōmi Province or Mount Matsunoo (松尾) in Yamashiro Province.",

      "source_id": "oyamakui-s1",

      }
    },
    {
      "id": "oyamakui-c05",
      "source_id": "oyamakui-s1",
      "quote": "They are the kami of mountains and good health.",
      "locator": "Article text",

      "source_id": "oyamakui-s1",
      "quote": "He is worshipped at Hie Sha (Hie Shrine, 日吉社, now Hiyoshi Taisha) and its network of affiliated shrines, including Hie Shrine in Edo.",

    "claim_ids": [

    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Sannō Gongen adalah dewa sinkretis yang memadukan pemujaan Gunung Hiei, Shinto, dan Buddha Tendai. Ia adalah dewa pelindung Enryakuji dan sebutan bagi dewa kuil Hiyoshi.",
      "en": "Sannō Gongen is a syncretic deity combining Mount Hiei worship, Shinto and Tendai Buddhism. He is the tutelary deity of Enryakuji and a name for Hiyoshi shrine deities.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "sanno-gongen-c01",
        "sanno-gongen-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Pemujaannya menyebar melalui kuil yang terkait dengan Hiyoshi. Saichō konon membangun pemujaannya setelah belajar di Tiongkok.",
      "en": "His worship spread through shrines affiliated with Hiyoshi. Saichō is said to establish the worship following his study in China.",
      "claim_ids": [

        "sanno-gongen-c04"
      ]

      "publisher": "Wikipedia",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "sanno-gongen-c01",
      "source_id": "sanno-gongen-s1",
      "quote": "山王権現（さんのうごんげん）は日枝山（比叡山）の山岳信仰と神道、天台宗が融合した神仏習合の神である。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sannō Gongen adalah dewa sinkretis yang memadukan pemujaan Gunung Hiei, Shinto, dan Buddha Tendai.",
        "en": "Sannō Gongen is a syncretic deity combining Mount Hiei worship, Shinto and Tendai Buddhism."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sanno-gongen-c02",
      "source_id": "sanno-gongen-s1",
      "quote": "山王権現とは、日枝山（比叡山）の、山岳信仰、神道、天台宗が融合して成立した、延暦寺の鎮守神である。また、日吉社の祭神を指すこともあった。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia adalah dewa pelindung Enryakuji dan sebutan bagi dewa kuil Hiyoshi.",
        "en": "He is the tutelary deity of Enryakuji and a name for Hiyoshi shrine deities."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sanno-gongen-c03",
      "source_id": "sanno-gongen-s1",
      "quote": "山王権現は、比叡山の神として「ひよっさん（日吉さん）」とも呼ばれ、日吉社を総本宮とする、全国の比叡社（日吉社）に祀られた。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pemujaannya menyebar melalui kuil yang terkait dengan Hiyoshi.",
        "en": "His worship spread through shrines affiliated with Hiyoshi."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sanno-gongen-c04",
      "source_id": "sanno-gongen-s1",
      "quote": "唐から帰国した最澄は、天台山国清寺に倣って比叡山延暦寺の鎮守神（地主神）として山王権現を祀った。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Saichō konon membangun pemujaannya setelah belajar di Tiongkok.",
        "en": "Saichō is said to establish the worship following his study in China."
      }

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Sarugami menunjuk perwujudan monyet sebagai utusan dewa dan yōkai monyet. Pemujaan Sannō memuliakan monyet sebagai utusan dewa dan makhluk gunung. Kumpulan cerita abad pertengahan juga menggambarkan yōkai monyet yang berbahaya.",
      "en": "Sarugami denotes monkey manifestations as divine messengers and monkey yōkai. Sannō worship reveres monkeys as divine messengers and mountain beings. Medieval story collections also portray harmful monkey yōkai.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "sarugami-c01",
        "sarugami-c02",
        "sarugami-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Banyak legenda berkisah tentang monyet yang menuntut kurban perempuan sebelum dikalahkan. Penggambaran belakangan memberi mereka kemampuan berbicara dan kadang pakaian manusia. Kisah itu menempatkan mereka di gunung jauh dari permukiman.",
      "en": "Many legends concern a monkey demanding a woman as sacrifice before being defeated. A later depiction gives them speech and sometimes human clothing. That account places them in mountains away from settlements.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "sarugami-c04",
        "sarugami-c05",
        "sarugami-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],

      "quote": "猿神（さるがみ）は、日吉神などの太陽神の使者とされるサルの化身、および中世の日本の説話に登場するサルの妖怪。",
      "locator": "Article text",

      "statement": {
        "id": "Sarugami menunjuk perwujudan monyet sebagai utusan dewa dan yōkai monyet.",

      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sarugami-c02",
      "source_id": "sarugami-s1",
      "quote": "中世から近世にかけて流行した山王信仰においてもサルは神の使いとしての役割を担っており、山の神としても尊ばれた。",
      "locator": "Article text",

      "id": "sarugami-c03",
      "source_id": "sarugami-s1",
      "quote": "中世の日本の説話集『今昔物語集』『宇治拾遺物語』などには、猿神は人間に害を為す妖怪として登場しており、中でも『今昔物語集』巻26「美作國神依猟師謀止生贄語」がよく知られている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kumpulan cerita abad pertengahan juga menggambarkan yōkai monyet yang berbahaya.",
        "en": "Medieval story collections also portray harmful monkey yōkai."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sarugami-c04",
      "source_id": "sarugami-s1",
      "quote": "これらの他にも日本各地に猿神退治の伝説があるが、内容は同様に、サルが人間の女性を生贄を求め、通りすがりの猟師や僧侶が身代りとなって退治するというものである。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Banyak legenda berkisah tentang monyet yang menuntut kurban perempuan sebelum dikalahkan.",
        "en": "Many legends concern a monkey demanding a woman as sacrifice before being defeated."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sarugami-c05",
      "source_id": "sarugami-s2",
      "quote": "They can speak, and sometimes they are seen wearing human clothes as well.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Penggambaran belakangan memberi mereka kemampuan berbicara dan kadang pakaian manusia.",
        "en": "A later depiction gives them speech and sometimes human clothing."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sarugami-c06",
      "source_id": "sarugami-s2",
      "quote": "They live in the mountains and tend to stay away from human-inhabited areas.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah itu menempatkan mereka di gunung jauh dari permukiman.",
        "en": "That account places them in mountains away from settlements."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
  "gaps": [
    {
      "field": "images",
      "searched": "Belum ada berkas Wikimedia Commons yang diperiksa untuk entri ini."
    }
  ]
}

  "short_description": {

    "claim_ids": [
      "shirouneri-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Shirouneri adalah yōkai Jepang yang digambar Sekien. Gambarnya menyerupai naga yang terbuat dari kain lusuh. Sekien menggambarkannya sebagai kain lap tua yang berubah.",
      "en": "Shirouneri is a Japanese yōkai illustrated by Sekien. The image resembles a dragon made from ragged cloth. Sekien describes it as an old wiping cloth transformed.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "shirouneri-c01",
        "shirouneri-c02",
        "shirouneri-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Namanya ditafsirkan sebagai permainan kata atas Shiroururi dalam Tsurezuregusa. Buku belakangan menambahkan serangan, lendir, dan bau busuk pada sosok itu. Kisah modern memperluas serangan menjadi kain berlendir yang mencekik pelayan.",
      "en": "Its name is interpreted as wordplay on Shiroururi in Tsurezuregusa. Later books add attacks, slime and foul odor to the figure. A modern account expands the attacks into a slimy cloth choking servants.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "shirouneri-c04",
        "shirouneri-c05",
        "shirouneri-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,
  "story_mode": null,
  "did_you_know": null,
  "abilities": [],

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "shirouneri-c01",
      "source_id": "shirouneri-s1",
      "quote": "白溶裔、白容裔、白うねり（しろうねり）は、鳥山石燕の妖怪画集『百器徒然袋』にある日本の妖怪。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Shirouneri adalah yōkai Jepang yang digambar Sekien.",
        "en": "Shirouneri is a Japanese yōkai illustrated by Sekien."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "shirouneri-c02",
      "source_id": "shirouneri-s1",
      "quote": "ぼろい布でできた竜のような姿で描かれ、「容裔」（ようえい）という熟語は「風でものがなびく様子」を示す。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Gambarnya menyerupai naga yang terbuat dari kain lusuh.",
        "en": "The image resembles a dragon made from ragged cloth."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "shirouneri-c03",
      "source_id": "shirouneri-s1",
      "quote": "石燕による解説文には「古きふきんの化けたるもの」とあり、古い布巾が化けたものとされる。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sekien menggambarkannya sebagai kain lap tua yang berubah.",
        "en": "Sekien describes it as an old wiping cloth transformed."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "shirouneri-c04",
      "source_id": "shirouneri-s1",
      "quote": "名称は兼好法師の『徒然草』に登場する人物「しろうるり」をもじって名づけられたものであり、石燕の創作による妖怪であると考えられている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Namanya ditafsirkan sebagai permainan kata atas Shiroururi dalam Tsurezuregusa.",
        "en": "Its name is interpreted as wordplay on Shiroururi in Tsurezuregusa."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "shirouneri-c05",
      "source_id": "shirouneri-s1",
      "quote": "昭和・平成以降の妖怪関連の書籍では、「白うねり」あるいは「白溶裔」と表記され、古い雑巾や布巾がこの妖怪に変化し、人間を襲い、体を覆う不快な粘液と全身から放つ悪臭で人を気絶させるという解説がしばしば見られるが、これは小説家・山田野理夫の著書『東北怪談の旅』に収録されている「古ぞうきんの仇討ち」における古雑巾の行動などが引用されていると見られている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Buku belakangan menambahkan serangan, lendir, dan bau busuk pada sosok itu.",
        "en": "Later books add attacks, slime and foul odor to the figure."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "shirouneri-c06",
      "source_id": "shirouneri-s2",
      "quote": "It attacks them by wrapping its slimy, mildewy body around their necks and heads, causing them to pass out from the stench.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah modern memperluas serangan menjadi kain berlendir yang mencekik pelayan.",
        "en": "A modern account expands the attacks into a slimy cloth choking servants."
      }
    }

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Suzuri-no-Tamashi adalah yōkai batu tinta dalam kumpulan Sekien.",
    "en": "Suzuri-no-Tamashi is an inkstone yōkai in Sekien’s collection.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "suzuri-no-tamashi-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Suzuri-no-Tamashi adalah yōkai batu tinta dalam kumpulan Sekien. Ilustrasinya memperlihatkan prajurit kecil di batu tinta. Pembaca kisah Heike melihat laut dan pertempuran di dalam batu tinta.",
      "en": "Suzuri-no-Tamashi is an inkstone yōkai in Sekien’s collection. The illustration shows tiny warriors on an inkstone. A reader of the Heike tale sees a sea and a battle inside the inkstone.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "suzuri-no-tamashi-c01",
        "suzuri-no-tamashi-c02",
        "suzuri-no-tamashi-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Satu tafsir menghubungkan batu tinta dengan arwah Taira yang tidak tenang. Kisah belakangan mengaitkan suara laut, pertempuran, dan jeritan dengan batu tinta. Kisah itu menambahkan gelombang, perahu kecil, dan prajurit yang muncul dari tinta.",
      "en": "One interpretation associates the inkstone with the restless Taira dead. A later account attributes sea sounds, battle noise and cries to the inkstone. That account adds waves, tiny boats and soldiers emerging from ink.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "suzuri-no-tamashi-c04",
        "suzuri-no-tamashi-c05",
        "suzuri-no-tamashi-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,
  "story_mode": null,
  "did_you_know": null,
  "abilities": [],
  "weaknesses": [],
  "variants": [],
  "stories": [],

  "learning_questions": [],
  "images": [],
  "sources": [
    {
      "id": "suzuri-no-tamashi-s1",
      "published": null,
      "accessed": "2026-10-01",

      "title": "硯の魂",
      "publisher": "Wikipedia",

      "type": "wikipedia",
      "author": null
    },
    {
      "id": "suzuri-no-tamashi-s2",
      "published": null,
      "accessed": "2026-10-01",
      "url": "https://yokai.com/suzurinotamashi/",
      "title": "Suzuri no tamashii",
      "publisher": "Yokai.com",
      "language": "en",
      "type": "encyclopedia",
      "author": "Matthew Meyer"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "suzuri-no-tamashi-c01",
      "source_id": "suzuri-no-tamashi-s1",
      "quote": "硯の魂（すずりのたましい）は鳥山石燕の妖怪画集『今昔百鬼拾遺』にある妖怪。硯の精（すずりのせい）とも呼称される。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Suzuri-no-Tamashi adalah yōkai batu tinta dalam kumpulan Sekien.",
        "en": "Suzuri-no-Tamashi is an inkstone yōkai in Sekien’s collection."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "suzuri-no-tamashi-c02",
      "source_id": "suzuri-no-tamashi-s1",
      "quote": "墨をするための道具である硯（すずり）の上に小さな武士たちが描かれており、以下の解説文が添えられている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ilustrasinya memperlihatkan prajurit kecil di batu tinta.",
        "en": "The illustration shows tiny warriors on an inkstone."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "suzuri-no-tamashi-c03",
      "source_id": "suzuri-no-tamashi-s1",
      "quote": "赤間ヶ関産の石硯を文具として愛用していた者が、『平家物語』を読みながらまどろんでいると、硯の中に海が現れ、やがて源平の合戦のような様子になったという意味である。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pembaca kisah Heike melihat laut dan pertempuran di dalam batu tinta.",
        "en": "A reader of the Heike tale sees a sea and a battle inside the inkstone."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "suzuri-no-tamashi-c04",
      "source_id": "suzuri-no-tamashi-s1",
      "quote": "赤間ヶ関（山口県下関市）は源平合戦における平家の終焉の地であり、かつて下関での壇ノ浦の戦いで滅びた平家の怨霊が硯に宿ったものであるなどと推察されている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Satu tafsir menghubungkan batu tinta dengan arwah Taira yang tidak tenang.",
        "en": "One interpretation associates the inkstone with the restless Taira dead."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "suzuri-no-tamashi-c05",
      "source_id": "suzuri-no-tamashi-s2",
      "quote": "When used, they produce sounds like the echo of the sea, the din of battle, and the screams of warriors.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah belakangan mengaitkan suara laut, pertempuran, dan jeritan dengan batu tinta.",
        "en": "A later account attributes sea sounds, battle noise and cries to the inkstone."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "suzuri-no-tamashi-c06",
      "source_id": "suzuri-no-tamashi-s2",
      "quote": "The ink inside begins to ripple and billow like the sea’s waves, and tiny boats and soldiers materialize out of the ink.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah itu menambahkan gelombang, perahu kecil, dan prajurit yang muncul dari tinta.",
        "en": "That account adds waves, tiny boats and soldiers emerging from ink."
      }
    }
  ],

      "en": "Tagitsuhime is a Japanese goddess among the Munakata trio. She is born from mist produced during Amaterasu and Susanoo’s oath.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "tagitsuhime-c01",
        "tagitsuhime-c02"
      ]

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "tagitsuhime-c01",
      "source_id": "tagitsuhime-s1",
      "quote": "タギツヒメ（タキツヒメとも）は、日本神話に登場する女神で、宗像三女神の一柱である。『古事記』では多岐都比売命、『日本書紀』では湍津姫命と表記される。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tagitsuhime adalah dewi Jepang dalam tiga dewi Munakata.",
        "en": "Tagitsuhime is a Japanese goddess among the Munakata trio."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tagitsuhime-c02",
      "source_id": "tagitsuhime-s1",
      "quote": "アマテラスとスサノオの誓約の段で、アマテラスがスサノオの十束剣を口に含み、三つにかみ砕き吐き出した霧から宗像三女神を生み、スサノオの物実から生まれたのでスサノオの子であると宣言された。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia lahir dari kabut yang muncul saat sumpah Amaterasu dan Susanoo.",
        "en": "She is born from mist produced during Amaterasu and Susanoo’s oath."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tagitsuhime-c03",
      "source_id": "tagitsuhime-s1",
      "quote": "『古事記』・『日本書紀』第二の一書では、3番目に化生し、辺津宮に祀られる。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kojiki menempatkannya pada kelahiran ketiga dan kuil Hetsumiya.",
        "en": "The Kojiki assigns her the third birth and Hetsumiya shrine."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tagitsuhime-c04",
      "source_id": "tagitsuhime-s1",
      "quote": "『日本書紀』本文・第一の一書・第三の一書では、2番目に化生し、中津宮に祀られる。 宗像大社では『日本書紀』本文の通り「湍津姫神」として宗像市大島の中津宮に祀られている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Versi Nihon Shoki dan pemujaan Munakata kini menempatkannya di Nakatsumiya.",
        "en": "Nihon Shoki versions and current Munakata worship place her at Nakatsumiya."
      }
    }

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Taka-nyūdō adalah yōkai biksu tinggi dari Shikoku dan Kinki.",
    "en": "Taka-nyūdō is a tall-monk yōkai of Shikoku and Kinki.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "taka-nyudo-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Taka-nyūdō adalah yōkai biksu tinggi dari Shikoku dan Kinki. Kisah Hyōgo menempatkannya di lorong sempit pembuat sake. Ia tumbuh menuju langit ketika pengamat menengadah.",
      "en": "Taka-nyūdō is a tall-monk yōkai of Shikoku and Kinki. A Hyōgo account places it in narrow sake-brewery alleys. It grows toward the sky as the observer looks up.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "taka-nyudo-c01",
        "taka-nyudo-c02",
        "taka-nyudo-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia dibandingkan dengan mikoshi-nyūdō dan Takabōzu. Tradisi Kagawa memakai membungkuk dan ucapan agar ia menghilang. Kisah Hyōgo mengidentifikasinya sebagai tanuki atau rubah yang berubah.",
      "en": "It is compared with mikoshi-nyūdō and Takabōzu. A Kagawa tradition uses a bow and a saying to make it disappear. Hyōgo accounts identify it as a transformed tanuki or fox.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "taka-nyudo-c04",
        "taka-nyudo-c05",
        "taka-nyudo-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,
  "story_mode": null,

      "id": "taka-nyudo-s1",

      "accessed": "2026-10-01",

      "title": "Taka-nyūdō",
      "publisher": "Wikipedia",
      "language": "en",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "author": null
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "taka-nyudo-c01",
      "source_id": "taka-nyudo-s1",
      "quote": "Taka-nyūdō (高入道, literally \"Tall Priest\" or \"Tall Monk\") is a yōkai transmitted primarily in Shikoku and the Kinki region.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Taka-nyūdō adalah yōkai biksu tinggi dari Shikoku dan Kinki.",
        "en": "Taka-nyūdō is a tall-monk yōkai of Shikoku and Kinki."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "taka-nyudo-c02",
      "source_id": "taka-nyudo-s1",
      "quote": "In Hyōgo, it is said to have appeared in the narrow alleyways of sake breweries.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah Hyōgo menempatkannya di lorong sempit pembuat sake.",
        "en": "A Hyōgo account places it in narrow sake-brewery alleys."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "taka-nyudo-c03",
      "source_id": "taka-nyudo-s1",
      "quote": "It would appear suddenly in front of people; as the person involuntarily looked up, the creature would grow taller and taller, reportedly becoming a giant that reached the heavens.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia tumbuh menuju langit ketika pengamat menengadah.",
        "en": "It grows toward the sky as the observer looks up."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "taka-nyudo-c04",
      "source_id": "taka-nyudo-s1",
      "quote": "It is a yōkai belonging to the same category as the Mikoshi-nyūdō, which possesses similar characteristics, and there is a theory that it is the same yōkai as the similarly named Takabōzu.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia dibandingkan dengan mikoshi-nyūdō dan Takabōzu.",
        "en": "It is compared with mikoshi-nyūdō and Takabōzu."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "taka-nyudo-c05",
      "source_id": "taka-nyudo-s1",
      "quote": "In Nawa, Kagawa Prefecture, it is said that if one bows without looking up and chants \"I lost, I saw past you\" (Maketa, mikoshita), it will disappear.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tradisi Kagawa memakai membungkuk dan ucapan agar ia menghilang.",
        "en": "A Kagawa tradition uses a bow and a saying to make it disappear."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "taka-nyudo-c06",
      "source_id": "taka-nyudo-s1",
      "quote": "Regarding its true identity, in Hyōgo, it is said to be a shapeshifted Tanuki (raccoon dog) or Kitsune (fox).",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah Hyōgo mengidentifikasinya sebagai tanuki atau rubah yang berubah.",
        "en": "Hyōgo accounts identify it as a transformed tanuki or fox."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],

    {

      "id": "takehazuchi-c01",
      "source_id": "takehazuchi-s1",
      "quote": "天羽槌雄神（あまのはづちおのかみ）は、日本神話の神である。 == 概要 == 『古語拾遺』において天羽槌雄神とされるほか、『日本書紀』では建葉槌命（たけはづちのみこと）、倭文神（しとりがみ）とされる。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {

    {
      "id": "takehazuchi-c02",
      "source_id": "takehazuchi-s1",
      "quote": "天照大神を天の岩戸から誘い出すために、文布（あや）を織ったとされる。文布は倭文布とも倭文とも書き、「シドリ」また「シヅリ」という織物である。",

      "context": "religious-tradition",

    },
    {
      "id": "takehazuchi-c03",
      "source_id": "takehazuchi-s1",
      "quote": "同じ織物の神では栲幡千々姫命、天棚機姫命が挙げられるが、天羽槌雄神は機織りの祖神とされている。",

      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia dianggap sebagai dewa leluhur penenunan.",

      "id": "takehazuchi-c04",
      "source_id": "takehazuchi-s1",
      "quote": "天羽槌雄神は建葉槌命の名で『日本書紀』に登場した倭文神で、経津主神・武甕槌命では服従しなかった星神の香香背男（かがせお）を服従させた神とされる。",
      "locator": "Article text",

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Takehiratori adalah dewa Jepang yang disamakan dengan beberapa nama kronik melalui silsilah Izumo.",
    "en": "Takehiratori is a Japanese deity identified with several chronicle names through Izumo genealogies.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "takehiratori-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Takehiratori adalah dewa Jepang yang disamakan dengan beberapa nama kronik melalui silsilah Izumo. Satu tafsir membaca namanya sebagai burung pemberani yang melintasi batas. Kojiki menjadikannya anak Amenohohi dan leluhur beberapa garis keturunan daerah.",
      "en": "Takehiratori is a Japanese deity identified with several chronicle names through Izumo genealogies. One interpretation reads his name as a brave bird crossing a boundary. The Kojiki makes him Amenohohi’s child and an ancestor of several local lineages.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "takehiratori-c01",
        "takehiratori-c02",
        "takehiratori-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Nihon Shoki menyebutnya mengikuti tugas ayahnya ke bumi dan juga tidak mengirim laporan. Terjemahan universitas menegaskan Takehiratori sebagai anak Amenohohi. Catatan terjemahan menafsirkan take sebagai gagah, sementara hira tetap tidak pasti.",
      "en": "The Nihon Shoki says he follows his father’s earthly mission and likewise sends no report. The university’s translation confirms Takehiratori as Amenohohi’s child. The translation notes interpret take as stalwart while leaving hira uncertain.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "takehiratori-c04",
        "takehiratori-c05",
        "takehiratori-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,
  "story_mode": null,
  "did_you_know": null,
  "abilities": [],

  "sources": [
    {
      "id": "takehiratori-s1",
      "published": null,

      "url": "https://ja.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E5%BB%BA%E6%AF%94%E8%89%AF%E9%B3%A5%E5%91%BD",
      "title": "建比良鳥命",
      "publisher": "Wikipedia",
      "language": "ja",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "author": null
    },
    {
      "id": "takehiratori-s2",
      "published": null,
      "accessed": "2026-10-01",
      "url": "https://www.kokugakuin.ac.jp/assets/uploads/2023/03/Kenkyukaihatsusuisinkikou_kiyouNo15.pdf",
      "title": "Kenkyukaihatsusuisinkikou_kiyouNo15.pdf",
      "publisher": "Kokugakuin University",
      "author": null,
      "language": "en",
      "type": "primary-text"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "takehiratori-c01",
      "source_id": "takehiratori-s1",
      "quote": "建比良鳥命（タケヒラトリ）は、日本神話に登場する神。 == 概要 == 『古事記』では建比良鳥命、『日本書紀』では大背飯三熊之大人（オオソビノミクマノウシ）・武三熊之大人（タケミクマノウシ）・武日照命（タケヒナテル）・武夷鳥（タケヒナトリ） ・天夷鳥（アマノヒナトリ）、『新撰姓氏録』では天夷鳥命、天日名鳥命と表記される。これらの異名・異称の同定は出雲国造家として出雲大社の祭祀を受け継いだ千家家が伝える系譜書『出雲國造傳統略』に拠っている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Takehiratori adalah dewa Jepang yang disamakan dengan beberapa nama kronik melalui silsilah Izumo.",
        "en": "Takehiratori is a Japanese deity identified with several chronicle names through Izumo genealogies."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "takehiratori-c02",
      "source_id": "takehiratori-s1",
      "quote": "建比良鳥命の「建」は「勇猛な」、「比良」は、「縁（へり）」と同源であり、物の端・隣との境界の意と解し、名義は「勇猛な、異郷への境界を飛ぶ鳥」と考えられる。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Satu tafsir membaca namanya sebagai burung pemberani yang melintasi batas.",
        "en": "One interpretation reads his name as a brave bird crossing a boundary."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "takehiratori-c03",
      "source_id": "takehiratori-s1",
      "quote": "『古事記』では天照大御神と須佐之男命の誓約の段で、天之菩卑能命の子が建比良鳥命であり、出雲国造・无邪志国造・上菟上国造・下菟上国造・伊自牟国造・津島県直・遠江国造等の祖神であると記されている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kojiki menjadikannya anak Amenohohi dan leluhur beberapa garis keturunan daerah.",
        "en": "The Kojiki makes him Amenohohi’s child and an ancestor of several local lineages."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "takehiratori-c04",
      "source_id": "takehiratori-s1",
      "quote": "『日本書紀』では、葦原中国の平定の段で大背飯三熊之大人（武三熊之大人ともいう）が父の天穂日命に次いで葦原中国に派遣されたが、父と同様に何も報告して来なかったと記されている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nihon Shoki menyebutnya mengikuti tugas ayahnya ke bumi dan juga tidak mengirim laporan.",
        "en": "The Nihon Shoki says he follows his father’s earthly mission and likewise sends no report."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "takehiratori-c05",
      "source_id": "takehiratori-s2",
      "quote": "Among the deities born second was Amenohohi no mikoto. His child was Takehiratori no mikoto 建比良鳥命 (21).",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Terjemahan universitas menegaskan Takehiratori sebagai anak Amenohohi.",
        "en": "The university’s translation confirms Takehiratori as Amenohohi’s child."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "takehiratori-c06",
      "source_id": "takehiratori-s2",
      "quote": "Take means “stalwart.” The meaning of hira is unclear",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Catatan terjemahan menafsirkan take sebagai gagah, sementara hira tetap tidak pasti.",
        "en": "The translation notes interpret take as stalwart while leaving hira uncertain."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Tiga Dewi Munakata adalah trio dewi Jepang yang berpusat di Munakata Taisha.",

    "claim_ids": [

    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Tiga Dewi Munakata adalah trio dewi Jepang yang berpusat di Munakata Taisha. Asal mereka dari perlombaan sumpah terkait dengan pemujaan demi keselamatan perjalanan dan pelayaran. Mereka menjaga jalur laut menuju daratan Asia dan Semenanjung Korea.",
      "en": "The Three Goddesses of Munakata are a Japanese divine trio centered on Munakata Taisha. Their oath-contest origin is linked to worship for safe travel and navigation. They guard sea routes toward the Asian continent and Korean peninsula.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "three-goddesses-of-munakata-c01",
        "three-goddesses-of-munakata-c02",
        "three-goddesses-of-munakata-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Kuil kini menempatkan Tagorihime, Tagitsuhime, dan Ichikishimahime di tiga tempat suci. Kojiki menyebut trio itu sebagai dewa yang dimuliakan garis keturunan Munakata. Kojiki juga menyebut Ichikishimahime Sayoribime.",
      "en": "Current shrines assign Tagorihime, Tagitsuhime and Ichikishimahime to the three sanctuaries. The Kojiki names the trio as deities honored by the Munakata lineage. The Kojiki also calls Ichikishimahime Sayoribime.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "three-goddesses-of-munakata-c04",
        "three-goddesses-of-munakata-c05",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "three-goddesses-of-munakata-c01",
      "source_id": "three-goddesses-of-munakata-s1",
      "quote": "宗像三女神（むなかたさんじょしん）は、宗像大社（福岡県宗像市）を総本宮として、日本全国各地に祀られている三柱の女神の総称である。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tiga Dewi Munakata adalah trio dewi Jepang yang berpusat di Munakata Taisha.",
        "en": "The Three Goddesses of Munakata are a Japanese divine trio centered on Munakata Taisha."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "three-goddesses-of-munakata-c02",
      "source_id": "three-goddesses-of-munakata-s1",
      "quote": "記紀に於いてアマテラスとスサノオの誓約で生まれた女神らで宗像大神（むなかたのおおかみ）、道主貴（みちぬしのむち）とも呼ばれ、あらゆる「道」の最高神として航海の安全や交通安全などを祈願する神様として崇敬を集めている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Asal mereka dari perlombaan sumpah terkait dengan pemujaan demi keselamatan perjalanan dan pelayaran.",
        "en": "Their oath-contest origin is linked to worship for safe travel and navigation."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "three-goddesses-of-munakata-c03",
      "source_id": "three-goddesses-of-munakata-s1",
      "quote": "この三女神は、日本から大陸及び古代朝鮮半島への海上交通の平安を守護する神。海北道中の島々（沖津宮・沖ノ島、中津宮・筑前大島）と辺津宮・宗像田島に祀られ、大和朝廷によって古くから重視された神々。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mereka menjaga jalur laut menuju daratan Asia dan Semenanjung Korea.",
        "en": "They guard sea routes toward the Asian continent and Korean peninsula."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "three-goddesses-of-munakata-c04",
      "source_id": "three-goddesses-of-munakata-s1",
      "quote": "これが宗像大社が祀る、沖津宮の「田心姫神（タゴリヒメ）」、中津宮の「湍津姫神（タギツヒメ）」、辺津宮の「市杵島姫神（イチキシマヒメ）」である。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kuil kini menempatkan Tagorihime, Tagitsuhime, dan Ichikishimahime di tiga tempat suci.",
        "en": "Current shrines assign Tagorihime, Tagitsuhime and Ichikishimahime to the three sanctuaries."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "three-goddesses-of-munakata-c05",
      "source_id": "three-goddesses-of-munakata-s2",
      "quote": "These three deities are the threefold deity honored by the Munakata no kimi lineage (20).",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kojiki menyebut trio itu sebagai dewa yang dimuliakan garis keturunan Munakata.",
        "en": "The Kojiki names the trio as deities honored by the Munakata lineage."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "three-goddesses-of-munakata-c06",
      "source_id": "three-goddesses-of-munakata-s2",
      "quote": "Next appeared Ichikishimahime no mikoto 市寸嶋比売命 (7). This deity’s other name is Sayoribime no mikoto 狭依毗売命 (8).",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",

      "en": "Tsunuga Arashito is a legendary Korean prince in the Nihon Shoki. He is associated with the origin of the place name Tsuruga.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "tsunuga-arashito-c01",
        "tsunuga-arashito-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Satu cerita memberinya tanduk di dahi dan perjalanan laut ke Jepang. Cerita lain menghubungkannya dengan batu putih yang berubah menjadi gadis lalu dewa kuil.",

      "publisher": "Wikipedia",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "tsunuga-arashito-c01",
      "source_id": "tsunuga-arashito-s1",
      "quote": "都怒我阿羅斯等（つぬがあらしと、生没年不詳）は、『日本書紀』に伝わる古代朝鮮の人物で、加羅国王の息子と言う。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tsunuga Arashito adalah pangeran Korea legendaris dalam Nihon Shoki.",
        "en": "Tsunuga Arashito is a legendary Korean prince in the Nihon Shoki."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tsunuga-arashito-c02",
      "source_id": "tsunuga-arashito-s1",
      "quote": "意富加羅国（大伽耶/大加羅、おほからのくに、現在の韓国南部）の王子で、地名「敦賀（つるが）」の由来の人物といわれる。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia dikaitkan dengan asal nama tempat Tsuruga.",
        "en": "He is associated with the origin of the place name Tsuruga."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tsunuga-arashito-c03",
      "source_id": "tsunuga-arashito-s1",
      "quote": "1つ目として崇神天皇の時、額に角の生えた都怒我阿羅斯等が船で穴門から出雲国を経て笥飯浦に来着したという。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Satu cerita memberinya tanduk di dahi dan perjalanan laut ke Jepang.",
        "en": "One narrative gives him a horn on his forehead and a voyage to Japan."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tsunuga-arashito-c04",
      "source_id": "tsunuga-arashito-s1",
      "quote": "2つ目の所伝では、阿羅斯等が国にある時、黄牛の代償として得た白石が美しい童女と化したため、阿羅斯等は合（まぐわい）をしようとした。すると童女は阿羅斯等のもとを去って日本に行き、難波並びに豊国の国前郡の比売語曽社の神になったという。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Cerita lain menghubungkannya dengan batu putih yang berubah menjadi gadis lalu dewa kuil.",
        "en": "Another narrative links him with a white stone that turns into a maiden and then a shrine deity."
      }
    }
  ],

      "id": "wd-q11298280-c03",
      "source_id": "wd-q11298280-s1",
      "quote": "梟（ふくろう）に似た姿だが、顔は人間のようで、耳と四つの目を持っている。「顒」という名称は、その鳴き声から来ている。 === 日照りの凶兆 === この怪鳥が現れた土地は旱魃に見舞われる。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Namanya meniru suaranya dan kemunculannya meramalkan kekeringan.",
        "en": "Its name imitates its cry and its appearance foretells drought."
      }
    }
  ],

      "quote": "負屓（ふき、拼音：Fùxì）は、李東陽が著した『懐麓堂集』の説による竜生九子の一つ。文章の読み書きを好み、故に石碑の頂に絡み付いているという。",

      "source_id": "wd-q757989-s1",
      "quote": "『贔』の字は財貨が多い様を表し、『屓』の字は『贔』に『尸』を付けたし、財宝を抱え込んでしまう意がある。つまり、『負屓』を字義通りに解釈すると『重たい荷物を背負う』という意味になる。",

      "en": "Huan is a legendary Chinese animal born from imprisoned people’s distress.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wd-q8320871-c01"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Kisah itu memberinya tubuh besar mirip kambing, mata biru, dan kaki tertanam di tanah. Dongfang Shuo mengusir makhluk itu dengan arak ketika menghalangi perjalanan Kaisar Wu.",
      "en": "The account gives it a huge goatlike body, blue eyes and feet fixed in the ground. Dongfang Shuo dispels the creature with wine when it blocks Emperor Wu’s journey.",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "wd-q8320871-c01",
      "source_id": "wd-q8320871-s1",
      "quote": "䍺（かん）は、中国に伝わる伝説上の動物。監獄に閉じ込められた罪人達の憂いの気より誕生したとされる。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Huan adalah hewan legendaris Tiongkok yang lahir dari kesusahan orang yang dipenjara.",
        "en": "Huan is a legendary Chinese animal born from imprisoned people’s distress."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q8320871-c02",
      "source_id": "wd-q8320871-s1",
      "quote": "山羊をあわせた身体に青い目を持ち、身長は小さいもので7から8メートル、大きいものでは10mを超える。また足が地面にめり込んでおり、人間の力では動かせないとされる。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah itu memberinya tubuh besar mirip kambing, mata biru, dan kaki tertanam di tanah.",
        "en": "The account gives it a huge goatlike body, blue eyes and feet fixed in the ground."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q8320871-c03",
      "source_id": "wd-q8320871-s1",
      "quote": "漢代に武帝が巡幸した際、一行の前に巨大な山羊が現れ行く手を阻んだ。武帝の側近で博識で知られた東方朔（とうほうさく）は、それが䍺であることに気付き、䍺の憂いを晴らすために大量の酒をかけたところ䍺は消滅したという。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dongfang Shuo mengusir makhluk itu dengan arak ketika menghalangi perjalanan Kaisar Wu.",
        "en": "Dongfang Shuo dispels the creature with wine when it blocks Emperor Wu’s journey."
      }
    }
